# DECISIONS.md

One line per architectural decision: **date · decision · reason**. Append, never rewrite.
When we change our minds, add a new row that supersedes the old one and mark the old one
`SUPERSEDED by D-NN`. This file is the record an auditor — or a future session — reads to
find out *why*, not *what*.

Status values: `LOCKED` (do not relitigate without asking) · `OPEN` (decided but revisitable)
· `PENDING` (raised, not yet decided) · `SUPERSEDED`.

---

## Seeded from `CLAUDE.md` — 2026-08-01

These were already decided before the repository existed. Recorded here so later changes
have somewhere to land.

| # | Date | Decision | Reason | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-01 | 2026-08-01 | **Stack: Tauri v2 + React 19 + TypeScript (strict) + Vite.** Excel writing in Rust via `rust_xlsxwriter`. | ~5–10 MB installer vs Electron's 100 MB+ matters for distribution over a corporate network; 30–60 MB idle RAM matters for an app left open next to CAD and ERP clients. Rust is also where the fast, correct Excel writer lives. | LOCKED |
| D-02 | 2026-08-01 | **Electron + ExcelJS is the fallback**, decided by end of Phase 4, recorded here. Do not carry both. | Rust-side xlsx generation is the one part that could stall the project; a pre-agreed exit avoids an open-ended stall. | LOCKED |
| D-03 | 2026-08-01 | **One `A3LayoutDescriptor` feeds both the preview and the export**, produced by a single pure function. Never write layout logic twice. | Two independent implementations diverge within a week. | LOCKED |
| D-04 | 2026-08-01 | **Rust contains zero layout logic** — it is a dumb serializer of the descriptor. | Keeps the WYSIWYG guarantee in one testable place, in the language where the tests are cheapest to write. | LOCKED |
| D-05 | 2026-08-01 | **Charts render in the frontend, export as PNG, embed into Excel as images.** Not native Excel chart objects. | Native Excel charts guarantee divergence between preview and file. | LOCKED |
| D-06 | 2026-08-01 | **Persistence is a `.ppsx` zip container, local only.** No server, no accounts, no cloud. | Portable, emailable, keeps images with the project, no database dependency for sharing. | LOCKED |
| D-07 | 2026-08-01 | **Every PPS method is a plugin** — `{ id, step, schema, Editor, renderToA3, readiness }`. Adding a method must never require touching the step page, the preview, or the exporter. | This is the core extensibility mechanism; §1.3 lists ~50 methods and the list will grow. | LOCKED |
| D-08 | 2026-08-01 | **`ProjectModel` is always 8-step. A template is a projection of the model onto a sheet, never the reverse.** | The supplied company forms are 7-step. Collapsing the model to fit a form loses the discipline the app exists to enforce. | LOCKED |
| D-09 | 2026-08-01 | **`reference/TEMPLATE_ANALYSIS.md` is authoritative for sheet geometry** — do not eyeball it from the `.xls`. | Cell-exact geometry cannot be reliably re-derived by hand each time. *(See D-16: the document itself now needs source verification.)* | LOCKED |
| D-10 | 2026-08-01 | **Default template is `farplas-7step-tr`, then `farplas-7step-plus` once the fidelity test passes.** `pps-8step-auto` (Template B) is an option the user chooses, not an upgrade we impose. | An app that outputs a non-standard A3 does not get adopted, however much better the non-standard one is. | **SUPERSEDED IN PART by D-157** — the rationale stands and is what moved the default; only the choice of *which* template it points at changed (P-29 closed 2026-08-05). |
| D-11 | 2026-08-01 | **Fishbone belongs to Step 4, not Step 2.** Step 2 is data-based stratification and localization. | Offering fishbone in Step 2 teaches the method wrong. | LOCKED |
| D-12 | 2026-08-01 | **Step 3 (Set a Target) is mandatory.** | It is the step that distinguishes Toyota's 8 steps from Imai's 7, and the one that gets dropped in practice. *(Enforcement mechanism unresolved — see D-19.)* | LOCKED |
| D-13 | 2026-08-01 | **AI is bring-your-own-key** (Anthropic, OpenAI, Google). We never hold a key, never proxy through our own server, never ship a default key. | Business decision, not an engineering one. Reversing it is a business decision too. | **SUPERSEDED IN PART by D-199** — the "never hold a key, never proxy through our own server, never ship a default key" principle stands unchanged; only the enumerated provider list was wrong for the real deployment. Farplas can only use its own contracted AI gateway, Vorion — not Anthropic/OpenAI/Google directly (2026-08-30). |
| D-14 | 2026-08-01 | **Keys live in the OS keychain and never enter the webview.** Every provider call originates in Rust. | A key that never reaches the renderer cannot be exfiltrated by anything running in the renderer. Consequence: the Tauri CSP needs no `connect-src` entries for provider domains. | LOCKED |
| D-15 | 2026-08-01 | **The assistant proposes; the human accepts.** No code path from a model response to `ProjectModel` bypasses an explicit Accept. No "fill the whole A3" button, ever. | An AI that fills in the A3 produces a beautiful document, zero learning, and a root cause that is plausible and wrong. This is architectural, not a UX preference. | LOCKED |
| D-16 | 2026-08-01 | **Critique before draft.** Default assistant mode is Socratic; drafting is always the second move. | Same reason as D-15. | LOCKED |
| D-17 | 2026-08-01 | **The model emits specs, not pictures.** Charts, tables and diagrams return as structured data rendered through the existing pipeline. Never AI-generated images. | An AI-produced image is an opaque artifact nobody can edit and nothing can verify; it would break D-03. | LOCKED |
| D-18 | 2026-08-01 | **Provenance on every entry, always** — `origin`, model, prompt version, who accepted, when. | This is what makes the report defensible in an IATF audit. "The AI wrote it" is not an answer. | LOCKED |
| D-19 | 2026-08-01 | **Anything read from a file or returned by a model is data, not instructions.** Wrap untrusted content in delimited blocks; surface instruction-like text to the user instead of acting on it. | A supplier PDF or a spreadsheet cell can contain text addressed to the AI. | LOCKED |
| D-20 | 2026-08-01 | **The app is fully functional with AI off.** Offline is the base product, not a fallback. A Playwright suite runs the whole happy path with no keys configured and must stay green. | Phases 8–10 are additive; everything before them must keep working, and stay working after. | LOCKED |
| D-21 | 2026-08-01 | **Never hardcode model lists.** Fetch from each provider's models endpoint, cache 24 h, keep a small fallback in config. Verify every provider API detail against current docs before implementing. | Hardcoded model IDs go stale within months and produce confusing failures. | LOCKED |
| D-22 | 2026-08-01 | **Zod schemas are the source of truth for types** — derive TS types from schemas, not the reverse. TypeScript strict, no `any`, no `@ts-ignore`. | One definition of shape, validated at every boundary. | LOCKED |
| D-23 | 2026-08-01 | **All user-facing strings go through i18next from the moment they are written.** TR and EN keys added together. Coaching content lives in `src/content/coaching/{tr,en}/step-N.md` as data, not JSX. | Retrofitting i18n costs more than doing it from line one, and the coaching text is content that non-engineers must be able to edit. | LOCKED |
| D-24 | 2026-08-01 | **One phase at a time, per `SPEC.md` §6**; stop and show a runnable build at each phase boundary. Fresh session per phase. | An app this size drifts badly if built in one pass; clean context per phase is also the cheaper way to run it. | LOCKED |

The print mode turns an invisible failure into a visible one, and lets the user check the sheet whenever they want rather than at export time. | LOCKED |

Reusing the ENG table with a −1 offset drifts by ~0.8 pt across the sheet — small per row, visible against a fidelity diff. | LOCKED |
| D-38 | 2026-08-01 | **Step 3 renders as a three-zone horizontal strip inside its existing 153.75 pt band. `farplas-7step-plus` does NOT re-cut Step 3.** Zones: (A) prioritised target items, (B) baseline→target trajectory chart, (C) the SMART commitment in display type over one compact mono line carrying baseline · target · unit · due date · owner. Closes P-11. | A real filled example supplied by the user proves the band holds a substantial Step 3 at this height — the constraint was never the height, it was treating the box as a vertical form. Keeping Step 3 uncut preserves §6b's central claim that A′ is *"the standard form with its step 5 box divided in two"* at zero organisational cost; re-cutting a second block would make A′ a new form requiring re-approval. Zone C's mono line is the addition that makes S3 verifiable — the supplied example states metric and target value but leaves baseline and due date implicit in the chart and has no owner, which is exactly the gap S3 exists to catch. | LOCKED |

| D-39 | 2026-08-01 | **A completed company A3 is a canvas, not a spreadsheet — and we deliberately do not copy that.** Evidence in `docs/02_REAL_WORLD_FINDINGS.md`: five real reports carry 646–935 chars of cell text (most of it the blank template's own labels) and up to 92 floating drawing objects. `PPS Hachi` writes structured content into cells instead. | Structured cells are searchable, validatable, traceable and machine-readable on the `Data` sheet; a pasted screenshot is none of those, and it is why no existing A3 tool can answer an auditor's "show me the chain from problem to standard". The cost is that our output will not look like what people produce today — stated here so it is a decision, not a surprise at the Phase 4 review. | LOCKED |
| D-40 | 2026-08-01 | **Type is sized for the printed page, not inherited from the template.** `buildA3Layout` enforces a legibility floor: minimum printed size ÷ template fit scale = authored minimum. At `farplas-7step-tr`'s verified 41.5 %, an 8 pt printed floor means **≥ 19 pt authored** — not the template's 14 pt. | The template's 14 pt prints at 5.8 pt. Real users escape this by hand-sizing text boxes; we generate cells, so the floor has to be computed. Consequence: less text fits per block, which makes appendix overflow (`SPEC.md` §2.3) load-bearing rather than a nicety. Pairs with D-34 — the print preview is what makes a violation visible. | LOCKED |

Real practice annotates everywhere — it is how the current form carries emphasis. Restricting it to Step 1 would remove a habit users rely on without replacing it. | LOCKED |

Revisit only if the boundary graph grows past "two pure trees, everything else can import them." | OPEN |

Revisit once a real release process exists. | OPEN |

| D-46 | 2026-08-02 | **Repository lives at `github.com/ozgurgovem/pps-hachi`, private, under the personal account, not an org.** | No Farplas or company GitHub org exists yet for this project; a private personal repo unblocks CI without waiting on org setup. Revisit before any external collaborator needs access — private-personal repos don't support fine-grained team permissions the way an org does. | OPEN |

| D-47 | 2026-08-02 | **The identity is built on 八, and the mark is 八 inside a PDCA cycle ring — drawn in drafting-office line work, not brush calligraphy.** Three constraints on the Phase 1 design pass: (a) the ring reads as the PDCA cycle, optionally in the template's own four phase colours; (b) 八's two diverging strokes echo the A3's two columns, verified balanced at 49.7/50.3, so the mark and the sheet agree; (c) the brand accent must **not** be `FF0000`, which already means "Plan" on the sheet. Closes P-10. | 八 ties the name to the eight steps — the one thing distinguishing this method from the 7-step form the company already uses — so the mark should say that rather than decorate. The three references supplied (red disc + gold ring, koi forming 八, brush 八 over an enso) all read as Japanese-restaurant branding; `SPEC.md` §2.1 asks for the drawing office instead: measured grids, hairline rules, revision stamps, plotter-pen line work. Keeping 八 and dropping the brush satisfies both. A red brand would make red mean two things inside one product. | LOCKED |
| D-48 | 2026-08-02 | **CI (`ci.yml`) cancels superseded runs, caches cargo + npm, and skips entirely on documentation-only changes.** Concurrency group keyed on workflow+ref with `cancel-in-progress`; `Swatinem/rust-cache` with `cache-on-failure`; `paths-ignore` on `**.md`, `docs/**`, `reference/**`, `LICENSE` for both `push` and `pull_request`. | macOS GitHub Actions runners bill at 10x the Linux rate, and every push was running a full unsigned Tauri build on both platforms — twelve phases of that adds up fast. None of the four changes touch what gets tested, only when and how often. | OPEN |
| D-49 | 2026-08-02 | **Phase 1 design system implemented in `src/index.css` (tokens) and `src/ui/*` (Radix-based primitives), one revision after the plan Barış approved.** Six named color primitives — Vellum (paper), Graphite (ink), Non-Repro (decorative hairline/grid only, `#88AFD6`), Steel (functional borders — added during implementation because Non-Repro alone is 1.86:1 against Vellum, short of WCAG 1.4.11's 3:1 floor for meaningful UI boundaries), Indelible (the one bold accent, `#5F4470`), Kraft (secondary chrome) — plus Danger (`#A6303F`/`#E08A93`), a semantic flagged/error color kept deliberately separate from `--color-plan` (`FF0000`) so a UI error state never borrows the exported sheet's own "Plan" meaning. Typography: Archivo Narrow (display, tracked-out caps + tabular figures rather than relying on the typeface alone to read as drafting lettering), Public Sans (body), Martian Mono (data/mono) — all self-hosted via `@fontsource` (OFL), no CDN, no paid-license risk (the plan's National 2 Condensed was the aspirational pick; this ships the free-fallback family named in the same plan). Primitives use the unified `radix-ui` package (behavior only) styled entirely from tokens; `react-router` v7 with `createHashRouter` (not browser history — the built app loads from Tauri's local asset scheme, not an HTTP server able to rewrite nested paths); manual `[data-theme]` dark mode via a custom Tailwind v4 variant, not the `prefers-color-scheme` media query, so the explicit toggle SPEC.md §2.1 asks for actually works. `/gallery` route renders every primitive against both themes. | The originally approved plan's accent ("Grease Pencil", `#C97A2B`, orange) was sent for an independent aesthetic review (fable model) before implementation, per Barış's request. The review found it sat in the same perceptual family as the SPEC-banned terracotta `#D97757` — a user would see "warm orange accent on off-white paper" regardless of the exact hex, i.e. functionally the banned default with saturation dialed down. Indelible (violet, named for the copying/indelible pencil used on shop-floor tags and triplicate paperwork) replaced it: outside the orange family, outside the four PDCA phase hues, and outside blue (already rejected in the plan's own self-critique as the single most generic SaaS accent). Both revisions are corrections to the *plan*, not deviations from it — same method (name a real drafting/shop object, then pressure-test against "would a generic B2B app also land here"), carried one step further than the first pass caught on its own. | LOCKED (pending Barış's review of `/gallery`) |
| D-50 | 2026-08-02 | **`react-router` is pinned to v7 (7.18.2), not v8, despite `npm audit` flagging a high-severity advisory (GHSA-qwww-vcr4-c8h2) that v8.3.0 fixes.** | The advisory is a CSRF bypass in RSC (React Server Components) mode / server actions. This app is a pure client-side Tauri SPA — no server, no RSC, no `"use server"` anywhere in the stack — so the vulnerable code path cannot execute here. v8 was released very recently and pins a tighter React peer range (`>=19.2.7`); staying on the mature v7 line avoids taking on a fresh major version's stability risk for a routing decision this early, in exchange for an advisory that doesn't apply to this app's architecture. Revisit if the advisory's scope is later found to be broader than RSC mode, or when v8 has more runway. | OPEN |

### Phase 2 persistence — plan reviewed by an independent Opus pass before any code, 2026-08-02

The schema/migration/persistence design below was drafted, then sent to an independent architectural review (opus model, no visibility into the drafting) before implementation, because every phase from here to 12 reads and writes through this same `.ppsx` format — a mistake here compounds. The review found one issue that would have silently destroyed user data (D-51) and several structural gaps the draft plan hadn't addressed. Four were genuine product decisions, not engineering calls, and went to Barış rather than being decided unilaterally (D-58 through D-61), per `CLAUDE.md`'s "ask me, do not silently invent scope."

| D-51 | 2026-08-02 | **`ProjectModel` Zod schemas are loose (`.passthrough()`/`.loose()`), never stripping unknown keys. Any additive schema change ships with a `schemaVersion` bump.** | Zod object schemas strip unrecognised keys by default. Since "validate the parsed result, then save that" was the planned open/save flow, a field added by a newer build is silently deleted the moment an older build opens and re-saves the file — no error, no warning. This is the exact failure `docs/01_ORIENTATION_REVIEW.md` §1.5 already flagged for `Entry.payload`; it turns out to apply to the whole model, and the validation step itself is what causes it. Caught by the Opus review before any schema was written. | LOCKED |
| D-52 | 2026-08-02 | **`Entry.payload` is `z.unknown()` at the `ProjectModel` level. Project load never inspects or validates it — that is a method-plugin concern at edit/render time. Closes the structural half of P-05; the UI half (read-only placeholder for an unknown `methodId`) moves to Phase 3.** | P-05 was tagged "Blocking: Phase 2" and the original Phase 2 draft plan didn't address it — at Phase 2 the method registry is empty, so validating payload shape at load time would refuse every project with any entry at all. Splitting it lets Phase 2 guarantee "an unknown `methodId` round-trips untouched" (a ~15-line fixture test) without needing the placeholder-card UI that only makes sense once the workspace exists. | LOCKED |
| D-53 | 2026-08-02 | **`StepState.readiness` is never persisted.** `ReadinessResult` is a derived selector return type living in `src/domain/readiness/`, not a field in the `src/domain/model/` schema. | `SPEC.md` §4.2 sketches `readiness` inside `StepState` but marks it "derived, not stored" in the same line — transcribing the sketch literally would persist it anyway. A stored readiness goes stale the moment a gate rule changes, and the file then disagrees with the app about whether a step passes, on a document whose whole purpose is to be defensible in an IATF audit. Already flagged in `docs/01_ORIENTATION_REVIEW.md` §1.10; carried forward here so it can't be missed again. | LOCKED |
| D-54 | 2026-08-02 | **`manifest.json` is the routing header — read first, structurally validated in Rust, decides migrate / refuse / open-read-only before `project.json` is touched. `project.json` is authoritative for content. A mismatch between the two (e.g. differing `id` or `schemaVersion`) is reported as a corrupt file, never silently reconciled.** | `SPEC.md` §4.1 and §4.2 both list `id` and `schemaVersion`, with no stated precedence — duplicated state with no precedence rule is a bug waiting for a hand-edited or partially-written file. This also answers where Rust may structurally validate: the manifest, never `project.json` (see D-55). | LOCKED |
| D-55 | 2026-08-02 | **Rust (`src-tauri/src/ppsx/`) is dumb about the `ProjectModel` shape — it never parses or validates `project.json`'s contents — but strict about the zip container itself: rejects path-traversal/absolute/symlink entries (`enclosed_name() == None` is a hard reject), caps total and per-entry uncompressed size and entry count, writes the UTF-8 general-purpose bit on entry names, and preserves unrecognised top-level zip members on re-save rather than dropping them.** | The "Rust stays dumb" instinct was right but under-argued: the real reason is D-22 (Zod is the source of truth for types — a hand-maintained serde mirror of `ProjectModel` in Rust would be a second schema definition maintained across a language boundary for eleven more phases), not the A3-descriptor precedent (D-04 exists because there are *two* renderers that must agree; there is only one `.ppsx` reader, so that reasoning doesn't transfer). But "dumb about the model" is not "careless about the archive" — `SPEC.md` §4.1 justifies `.ppsx` as "portable, emailable," meaning the app opens zip files that arrived from outside, and zip bombs / path traversal are container-level concerns that belong in Rust because that's where extraction happens. | LOCKED |
| D-56 | 2026-08-02 | **`.ppsx` writes are atomic: write to a temp file in the same directory as the target (not the system temp dir — cross-volume rename is a copy, not atomic), fsync, then rename over the target.** | Phase 2's done-condition is literally "a project survives quit and reopen with all fields intact," and there is no `history/` snapshot to recover from yet (that's Phase 3) — a crash or power loss mid-write against a naive truncate-and-write would total-loss the file in exactly the untested case. Windows needs `ReplaceFileW` or an equivalent persist-path, not delete-then-rename, which reopens the same window. | LOCKED |
| D-57 | 2026-08-02 | **Migration functions in `src/domain/migrations/` may not import the current model schema from `src/domain/model/` — enforced by extending the `no-restricted-imports` ESLint boundary (same mechanism as D-43). Each migration takes and returns `unknown`, narrows its own inputs, and embeds a frozen copy of whatever shape it depends on.** | A migration typed against the live schema silently changes meaning every time that schema is edited later — tests keep passing because they're reading the current schema, not the one the migration was written against. This is the most common way migration chains rot, and it's invisible without the lint rule. | LOCKED |
| D-58 | 2026-08-02 | **`Round` shape: every `Entry` carries a `roundId`; `Round` itself is metadata only (opened-at, reason, closed-at) — not a snapshot of step 4–7 entries.** Barış's decision after the Opus review, choosing the lower-risk of two options it raised. | `SPEC.md` §4.2 uses `rounds: Round[]` without ever defining `Round`, and its shape is genuinely structural — readers arrive in Phases 3, 4, 5, 7 and 10, so retrofitting the other design later is a rewrite of all of them. The `roundId`-on-`Entry` design keeps one entry list (no duplication, no consistency problem between a round snapshot and the live steps) and degrades to a no-op while `rounds` is empty, which is Phase 2 and 3's actual state. | LOCKED |
| D-59 | 2026-08-02 | **A `.ppsx` whose `manifest.schemaVersion` is newer than the running app understands opens read-only, with "Created by a newer version of PPS Hachi — update to edit," rather than being refused.** Barış's decision after the Opus review. | `CLAUDE.md` and `SPEC.md` are deliberately one-directional — "an older `.ppsx` must always open" — and say nothing about the reverse. But D-32 (admin-free per-user Windows installs, no centrally pushed updates) plus the updater not arriving until Phase 12 (`SPEC.md` §6) mean version skew is the *normal* condition for the entire Phase 2–11 build, not an edge case. A hard refusal would mean an unupdated reviewer can't read a report at all, which breaks the "portable, emailable" property D-06 exists for. Read-only is safe specifically because of D-51 (loose parsing) and D-54 (manifest read before content) — the save path simply never executes. | LOCKED |
| D-60 | 2026-08-02 | **The recent-projects index is a single JSON file in the platform app-local-data-dir (via Tauri's path API, atomic-written per D-56's pattern), not SQLite — despite `SPEC.md` §4.3 naming SQLite explicitly.** Barış's decision after the Opus review. | The index is a disposable cache/projection of the `.ppsx` files themselves, never a source of truth — the plan already treats a lost or corrupt index as "degrade to an empty recent list," not a recovery scenario. `tauri-plugin-sql` would pull `sqlx` and a full async runtime into the binary for a ~30-row table, cutting against D-01's own rationale for choosing Tauri over Electron (installer size, idle RAM), and would introduce a second migration mechanism in the same phase D-57 just made the first one deliberate about. SQLite earns its place once `SPEC.md` §1.3 Step 8's searchable knowledge-base requirement is real — revisit at Phase 7. | LOCKED |
| D-61 | 2026-08-02 | **"Import `.ppsx`" and "Open Existing Project" (`SPEC.md` §2.1) are the same action — opening a file from anywhere on disk. There is no app-managed library folder; the recent-index tracks paths and lazily prunes an entry when the file is no longer found there.** Barış's decision after the Opus review. | This determines the index design (path-keyed vs. a catalog of a directory the app owns) and was ambiguous in the spec. Per `CLAUDE.md`'s copy voice rule ("the same action keeps the same name through the whole flow"), the two labels for one action should probably collapse into one control at the UI-copy level too — worth revisiting when the launch screen ships, not a Phase 2 blocker. | LOCKED |
| D-62 | 2026-08-02 | **The Phase 2 migration scaffold ships as: a chain-contiguity invariant test (registered migrations form `1→2→…→CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` with no gaps or duplicates) plus a checked-in `.ppsx` fixture corpus generated by the real Phase 2 writer (minimal project, fully-populated project, Turkish text throughout, unknown `methodId`) — not an identity/no-op v1 migration step.** | An identity migration only proves an identity function is an identity function — it exercises nothing the real chain will ever do. What can't be recreated later is an *authentic* v1 file; once the format moves, any "v1 fixture" written from memory is a reconstruction of what v1 was believed to look like, and the first real migration ends up tested against that belief instead of reality. The fixtures cost nothing to check in now and are the actual asset every future migration validates against. | LOCKED |

### Phase 2 implementation — filled in while building against D-51 through D-62

| D-63 | 2026-08-02 | **"Import `.ppsx`" and "Open Existing Project" collapse into one launch-screen control** — resolves D-61's own copy-voice flag. | D-61 already named this as "worth revisiting when the launch screen ships." Both trigger `openProjectAtPath`; there is no second control, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "the same action keeps the same name through the whole flow." | OPEN |
| D-64 | 2026-08-02 | **`read_ppsx` explicitly rejects a raw entry name starting with `/`, `\`, or a drive letter (`looks_absolute` in `archive.rs`), on top of the `enclosed_name() == None` check D-55 specifies.** | The `zip` crate (v8.6.0) does not reject absolute-looking entry names the way D-55 assumed — its own doc comment says it deliberately *normalizes* a leading root/prefix into a relative path instead ("allows extraction of ZIP files with absolute paths, similar to other ZIP tools"), so `enclosed_name()` alone lets `/etc/passwd` through as `Some("etc/passwd")`. Verified empirically in `rejects_absolute_path_entry`. Anyone "simplifying" this check later by trusting `enclosed_name()` alone would silently reopen this gap. | LOCKED |
| D-65 | 2026-08-02 | **Launch-screen recent-list cache shape: `{ path, title, customer?, owner?, currentStep, lastModified }`, no thumbnail field.** `currentStep` is a count of steps with ≥1 entry, computed by `countStepsWithEntries` (`src/app/routes/projectProgress.ts`) — not the real readiness/gate system. | `SPEC.md` §2.1 lists title/customer/owner/step-progress/last-modified/thumbnail; a thumbnail needs the A3 renderer (Phase 4) and would be pure decoration with no real data behind it today. `currentStep` needs *some* definition now because `StepTick` needs a number, and the real readiness system (`src/domain/readiness/`) needs gate rules Phase 7 hasn't built yet — the entry-count proxy is honest about being provisional and costs nothing to replace later, since this is a disposable D-60 cache, not persisted `.ppsx` content. | OPEN |
| D-66 | 2026-08-02 | **D-57's `no-restricted-imports` migration boundary exempts `*.test.ts` files** — only production migration code may not import `src/domain/model`. | D-57's stated harm is a migration silently changing meaning when the live schema it's typed against is edited later. A *test* importing `CURRENT_SCHEMA_VERSION` to check the real registry against the real current version (D-62's contiguity guard) doesn't have that failure mode — a schema change breaking that test is the loud, correct signal, not the silent rot D-57 guards against. Verified both directions: a production file importing `../model` is rejected; the same import from a `.test.ts` file passes. | LOCKED |

### Post-implementation security review — independent Opus pass on `archive.rs` + `atomic.rs`, 2026-08-02

Requested after Phase 2 shipped, scoped to the two files handling untrusted external input (a `.ppsx` arriving by email, per D-06) and the atomic-write primitive. The review ran empirical PoCs rather than just reading the code — confirmed D-64's fix was sound but incomplete, found two issues ranking above it, and confirmed two things I'd reasoned about without being able to test (the Windows `rename` claim, and that `looks_absolute` genuinely has no TOCTOU/Unicode-normalization gap). Full findings are in the review transcript, not reproduced here; D-67/D-68 record the fixes actually made.

| D-67 | 2026-08-02 | **`read_ppsx` never trusts `entry.size()` (the zip's declared uncompressed size) for allocation or as the cap enforcement mechanism.** `read_capped` bounds actual bytes read via `.take(budget + 1)`, where `budget` is the tighter of the per-entry and remaining-total caps; the declared size is not consulted at all. A new pre-parse file-size ceiling (`file_size_ceiling` — real content budget plus a generous per-entry overhead allowance) rejects an implausibly large file *before* `ZipArchive::new()` unconditionally parses its central directory. `is_safe_relative_name` (the write-side check) is rewritten to split on both `/` and `\` regardless of host OS instead of using `std::path::Path`, and the read loop now runs every normalized name through that exact same predicate plus a duplicate-name check — so read and write share one definition of "safe name" instead of two that could drift. | CRITICAL, empirically verified: a 4-byte declared size decompressed to 200 MiB and was accepted under the old per-entry check, since it compared the *declared* size against the cap, never the actual bytes produced — flate2's decompressor does not clamp output to a declared size, and `Crc32Reader` validates the CRC only after `read_to_end` already returned. Separately, `is_safe_relative_name` using host-path semantics meant a Windows-style traversal name (`..\..\evil`) passed the write-side check on macOS/Linux — verified end to end that `write_ppsx` would emit a `.ppsx` containing that literal, unsafe entry name. Both traced to the same root cause: a check trusting something attacker-controlled (a declared size; the host OS's path parser) instead of measuring/normalizing directly. | LOCKED |
| D-68 | 2026-08-02 | **`atomic_write`'s temp file is created with `create_new` (O_EXCL-equivalent) under a name with a random component, never plain `create()`; the temp file is cleaned up on a failed `rename`, not just a failed write; the parent directory is best-effort `fsync`'d after a successful rename, on Unix.** | HIGH, empirically verified: the old temp name was `.{file}.tmp-{pid}` — fully predictable — and `File::create` follows an existing symlink. A symlink planted in advance at that path redirected the write to an arbitrary file, and the subsequent `rename` moved the *symlink itself* onto the project path, so every later save repeated the redirection. Realistic for this app specifically: `.ppsx` projects are expected to live on shared network drives (D-06's "portable, emailable"). The rename-failure leak and missing directory fsync were confirmed as real but lower-severity gaps in the same function while fixing the above. The Windows `rename` claim in the original D-56 comment was independently verified against the actual rustc 1.97.1 std source (`MoveFileExW` with `MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING`, falling back to `SetFileInformationByHandle` only on `ACCESS_DENIED`) and found correct — no change needed there beyond tightening the comment's wording. | LOCKED |

---

## Phase 3 — workspace, state, undo/autosave/history

### Plan reviewed by an independent Opus pass before implementation, 2026-08-02

`docs/01_ORIENTATION_REVIEW.md` §1.10 flagged this since Phase 0 and it sat unanswered:
undo (100-deep, command pattern), autosave (30 s), and history snapshots (last 20) are
three overlapping persistence mechanisms with no stated interaction. A draft design
(Zustand+immer; delta-command undo; autosave piggybacking history snapshots onto the
existing `otherEntries` zip round-trip) was sent to an independent architectural review
(opus model, no visibility into the drafting) before any code was written, the same
process Phase 2's persistence design went through. The review read the actual shipped
code rather than trusting the summary and found something the draft couldn't have caught
by construction: `otherEntries` round-tripping was already silently broken in shipped
Phase 2 code (D-79) — the same failure shape as D-51, one layer lower. It also found the
draft's history-via-`otherEntries` mechanism was the wrong owner for that responsibility
and recommended moving it into Rust as a sidecar instead, which reshaped several other
decisions below. Full findings are in the review transcript, not reproduced here;
D-69–D-84 record the decisions actually made. Per this session's explicit authorization
to proceed without pausing (contrast Phase 2's D-58–D-61, which went to Barış in real
time for the equivalent product-shaped calls), the items the review flagged as
product-shaped (D-74, D-75, D-77, D-83) were decided directly rather than gated on a
reply — flagged clearly here so Barış can override any of them on read-through.

| D-69 | 2026-08-02 | **State management is Zustand, without immer. `src/state/` holds only store wiring, the undo/redo stacks, the autosave scheduler and the keyboard handler — the reducer and derived selectors live in `src/domain/commands/` and `src/domain/selectors/`.** | Context was evaluated and rejected: a single Context holding the whole open project re-renders every consumer on every keystroke, and offers no clean handle for a `setInterval` autosave timer or a global `Ctrl+Z` listener living outside any component's render. Zustand is ~1 kB, matches the size-consciousness D-60 already established, and gives `getState()`/`subscribe()` for exactly that outside-React access. immer was dropped — the actual mutations this app performs (replace one entry in a step's array, insert/remove at an index, permute an array) are three-line spreads behind one `updateStep` helper, and immer's mutable-looking API is the exact exception `coding-style.md`'s immutability rule otherwise bans, for ~15 KB and an auto-freeze of the whole tree on every update. | LOCKED |
| D-70 | 2026-08-02 | **Undo is a single dispatcher (`applyCommand`) over JSON-serializable delta commands, 100-deep, in-memory only. Redo is cleared on any newly-dispatched command. Every command's `apply`/`invert` checks its precondition (target entry exists; for reorder, the id set matches exactly) and clears the stack with a surfaced error rather than half-applying. `src/domain/commands/` is the only code path allowed to mutate `ProjectModel` — no store action `set()`s the project directly.** | `SPEC.md` §4.3 literally says "command pattern", which the review reads as confirming deltas over full-`ProjectModel` snapshots — cheap regardless of project size, and (unlike a snapshot stack) a later non-undoable act is never reverted by undoing an earlier edit underneath it. The single-dispatcher rule is what makes the 100-deep invariant actually hold: without it, any direct mutation elsewhere silently invalidates every command below it on the stack the moment schema/notes/sign-off code is added in a later phase. | LOCKED |
| D-71 | 2026-08-02 | **`Entry.order` vs. array position: array position is authoritative in memory. Entries are sorted by `order` once on project load; every mutation ends by re-sequencing `order` to the array index, contiguous from 0.** | The two representations had no stated precedence — the same shape as the D-54 manifest/project duplication bug. Fixing this resolves the delete/reinsert-index collision question for free: reinsert at `min(index, len)` and re-sequence, and LIFO already guarantees the array is exactly where the paired command left it. | LOCKED |
| D-72 | 2026-08-02 | **Autosave uses a monotonic `revision` counter, incremented by the command dispatcher on every mutation. `dirty = revision !== lastSavedRevision`. A save captures `revisionAtStart` before writing and sets `lastSavedRevision = revisionAtStart` (not the revision current when the write finishes) on success only; a second save request while one is in flight sets a "save again" flag instead of racing a second `writePpsx` call. The 30 s timer and step-navigation trigger share one save function with a 5 s minimum interval between navigation-triggered saves. Read-only projects (D-59) are hard-gated inside the save function itself. On the window's close-requested event, a dirty project is flushed before the window is allowed to close.** | Without a revision guard, two orderings of "clear the dirty flag" both lose edits made during the write (a possibly multi-second call on a network share), and nothing serializes overlapping writers. The read-only gate belongs in the save function, not just the UI, because the autosave timer runs outside React and has no visibility into what the UI is hiding. Flush-on-close closes the ⌘Q-five-seconds-after-typing gap that autosave-only otherwise leaves open. | LOCKED |
| D-73 | 2026-08-02 | **The save indicator has four states — `saved · HH:MM` (from the completed write, localized through i18next), `saving…`, `unsaved changes`, and a sticky `not saved — <reason>` with retry — plus a distinct `read-only` state.** | A two-state (saved/unsaved) indicator can lie: a failed write, an edit landing mid-write, or a conflict (D-77) all need to be visibly different from a successful save, on a document whose whole purpose is to be defensible. Undoing back to the exact last-saved state is deliberately still "unsaved" under D-72's `revision`-based dirty check — the alternative (content hashing) would make the indicator claim "saved" for a state that was never actually the one written to disk. | LOCKED |
| D-74 | 2026-08-02 | **History snapshots live as a sidecar under app-local-data-dir (`history/{projectId}/{timestamp}.json`, Rust-side, D-56-style atomic writes), never inside the `.ppsx` itself — this corrects `SPEC.md` §4.1's `history/` entry in the container layout, the same way D-60 already overrode §4.3's SQLite mention.** | `SPEC.md` §4.1 puts `history/` in the zip. But D-06 justifies `.ppsx` as "portable, emailable" — twenty rolling snapshots traveling inside a file sent to a customer or an IATF auditor means every intermediate draft (a root cause that was wrong, a supplier named and then unnamed) ships with it, the same failure shape `docs/01_ORIENTATION_REVIEW.md` §1.10 already flagged for `ai-log.jsonl` (P-07). A sidecar also removes the write-side size-cap contention (D-78) and the write-amplification the review found in the original `otherEntries`-piggyback draft (serializing the whole project into the JSON IPC bridge on every 30 s tick). Product-shaped call, decided directly under this session's standing authorization — flagged for Barış's review. | LOCKED |
| D-75 | 2026-08-02 | **History snapshots are a manual, explicit rollback mechanism ("restore an earlier autosave point"), not automatic crash recovery. Phase 3 ships snapshot writing (on autosave, on project open, and immediately before any restore) plus a `restoreFromSnapshot` store action and its tests — the restore-picker UI is deferred; it is not part of Phase 3's three-band workspace spec.** | D-56's atomic writes already make the file on disk crash-safe — a torn write cannot happen. Snapshots buy almost nothing for the "crash recovery" framing `SPEC.md` §4.3 uses and a great deal for "I want to go back to before I rewrote Step 4," which is a different feature with different UI. Keeping the writing/restoring mechanism but deferring the picker keeps Phase 3 scoped to its actual done-condition while still answering the §1.10 interaction question architecturally. Product-shaped scope call, decided directly — flagged for Barış's review. | LOCKED |
| D-76 | 2026-08-02 | **Restoring a snapshot runs the exact same migrate → `ProjectModelSchema.safeParse` → id-equality pipeline `openProjectAtPath` runs (factored out of `openProjectFlow.ts` so both callers share it, never drift), then clears both the undo and redo stacks, then saves immediately so memory and disk agree.** | A `history/*.json` blob is untrusted JSON from a file (D-19) — treating it as an already-valid `ProjectModel` skips exactly the validation `openProjectAtPath` exists to do, and a snapshot written at an older `schemaVersion` restored raw would desync from the current manifest. Clearing both stacks (not just undo) follows from D-70: every command's delta was computed against a timeline that no longer exists post-restore. | LOCKED |
| D-77 | 2026-08-02 | **`ppsx_write` takes an optional `expectedModifiedMs`; `ppsx_read` returns the file's current mtime. A mismatch refuses the write with a distinct `Conflict` error (`archive.rs`) rather than overwriting in either direction. On conflict, autosave surfaces the D-73 sticky "not saved — file changed on disk" state; there is no automatic reload or merge in Phase 3.** | `.ppsx` files live on shared plant drives (D-06); autosave writing on a timer with no user action in the loop makes "two engineers, same file, at once" a real and not just theoretical failure — currently undetectable anywhere in the stack. The detection mechanism is an engineering call; the safest possible default response (refuse and warn, never silently pick a side) is the product-shaped part, decided directly here — richer reconcile/merge UX is left for a later pass, flagged for Barış's review. | LOCKED |
| D-78 | 2026-08-02 | **`write_ppsx` enforces the same entry-count / per-entry-size / total-size caps `read_ppsx` already enforces (D-67's `Limits`), checked against uncompressed content size before the zip is built.** | Nothing previously stopped the app from producing a `.ppsx` that `read_ppsx`'s own caps would then refuse to open — a file that writes successfully and is then permanently unopenable. Symmetric enforcement closes that gap outright. | LOCKED |
| D-80 | 2026-08-02 | **The base command shape (`src/domain/commands/types.ts`) carries `undoable: boolean` (default `true`) from Phase 3 on, even though nothing sets it `false` yet.** | A future non-undoable action (sign-off is the concrete example already in `SPEC.md` §4.2) needs the dispatcher to already understand the concept rather than retrofitting it into every existing command variant when that phase arrives. Forward-compatible scaffolding only — no sign-off logic is built now. | LOCKED |
| D-81 | 2026-08-02 | **Rust's `ppsx_write`/`ppsx_read` keep couriering `otherEntries` as raw bytes across the JSON IPC bridge in Phase 3 — the larger redesign the review raised (Rust owns add/remove-by-name, streams bytes instead of JSON-encoding them) is deferred.** | The review's concern was volume: a growing `otherEntries` array (images, chart PNGs, or — under the rejected draft — twenty history snapshots) serialized as a JSON number array on every save. With D-74 moving history out of the container and Phase 3 shipping no image import, `otherEntries` is empty for every Phase 3 project — the byte-courier cost the review warned about doesn't exist yet. Revisit when Phase 4/5 add image or chart assets; F-1's correctness fix (D-79) stands regardless of this deferral. | OPEN |
| D-82 | 2026-08-02 | **The method registry stores plugins with their payload type erased to `unknown` (`ErasedMethodPlugin`), via a single `registerMethod()` boundary function.** | `MethodPlugin<X>`'s `Editor` puts `X` in a contravariant (function-parameter) position, so TypeScript correctly refuses to let `MethodPlugin<X>` sit in an array typed `MethodPlugin<unknown>[]` — a real type error, not a strictness setting to relax. `registerMethod` is the one place a single `as unknown as` cast happens, safe by construction because a plugin only ever receives a payload that originated from itself (`createEmptyPayload`, or a value already round-tripped through its own `schema`). | LOCKED |
| D-83 | 2026-08-02 | **If the flush-on-close save (D-72) fails, the window close is blocked and an error is surfaced rather than closing silently.** | A silent save-and-quit is right when the save succeeds; when it fails (disconnected share, file locked by another program), closing anyway is the one path that guarantees data loss with no chance to retry. Product-shaped call, decided directly — flagged for Barış's review. | LOCKED |
| D-84 | 2026-08-02 | **Text-field edits write to the store on every keystroke (never debounced) so autosave/crash-safety is always lossless; only the *undo command* coalesces — consecutive edits to the same entry/field within ~600 ms mutate the top-of-stack command's `after` in place instead of pushing a new one. The window seals (next edit pushes fresh) on blur, focus change, entry switch, step navigation, any non-text command, undo/redo, restore, or save. Native browser undo is intercepted (`preventDefault`) inside entry text fields and routed to the project undo stack instead.** | Debouncing the *store* write (the tempting alternative) means a crash or autosave tick during the debounce window silently drops in-flight text and the D-73 indicator lies about having saved it. Coalescing only the undo entry keeps the 100-deep stack from filling with one command per keystroke while keeping persistence exact. Since inputs are controlled, the browser's native text-undo is already non-functional without this interception — the coalescing window becomes the de facto text-undo granularity. | LOCKED |

### `otherEntries` round-tripping was already silently broken — found in shipped Phase 2 code

| D-79 | 2026-08-02 | **`writePpsx`'s `otherEntries` parameter loses its `= []` default; every call site must now say explicitly what happens to the archive's existing non-`manifest.json`/`project.json` members. The open→edit→save flow threads `otherEntries` and `modifiedMs` from `ppsx_read`'s response through to the next `ppsx_write` call unchanged, and `OpenProjectOutcome`'s `opened` variant now carries both fields.** | D-55 already preserves every unrecognised zip member on the Rust side and was verified for it (`round_trips_unknown_top_level_entries_untouched`). But nothing on the TypeScript side ever read `raw.otherEntries` back out of a `ppsx_read` response — `openProjectFlow.ts` discarded it, and `ppsx_write`'s `otherEntries: ArchiveEntryPayload[] = []` default made calling it without them the path of least resistance. Phase 2 never triggered this because it never saved a project after opening it; Phase 3's autosave is the first code path that does. Same failure class as D-51 — validate/read, then persist only what you modeled — one layer lower, in the container instead of the JSON; found by the independent review reading shipped code, not by inspecting the Phase 3 draft. | LOCKED |

### Phase 3 implementation — filled in while building against D-69 through D-84

| D-85 | 2026-08-02 | **The left rail's completion state (`src/app/routes/workspace/stepStatus.ts`) computes only `empty`/`inProgress` this phase — `complete` and `flagged` are deferred.** | `SPEC.md` §2.2 lists four states, but `complete`/`flagged` both require the §1.2 gate rules (S1–S8), which don't ship until Phase 7 — `StepState.readiness` is deliberately never persisted (D-53) and no selector exists yet to derive it from. Same provisional-selector shape D-65 already used for the launch screen's `currentStep`. | OPEN |
| D-86 | 2026-08-02 | **Entry reordering ships two mechanisms that dispatch the same `entries.reorder` command: `@dnd-kit` drag-and-drop (the literal SPEC.md §2.2 ask) and explicit Move-up/Move-down buttons on every `EntryRow`.** | Drag alone is hard to drive deterministically from a keyboard or from RTL/jsdom tests; the buttons are the guaranteed-accessible, guaranteed-testable path, and dnd-kit's own keyboard sensor rides the same buttons' semantics. Because both go through `buildReorderCommand`, they can't disagree with each other. | OPEN |
| D-87 | 2026-08-02 | **The coach band's collapsed/expanded state persists per step in `localStorage`, keyed `pps-hachi.coachBand.step-N` — not in `ProjectModel`.** | It's a UI preference (SPEC.md §2.2: "remembers state per step"), not project content; putting it in the persisted model would mean it round-trips through every `.ppsx` a user ever receives from someone else, which makes no sense for a per-viewer reading preference. | OPEN |
| D-88 | 2026-08-02 | **"Jumping ahead from an empty step shows a gentle advisory" (`SPEC.md` §2.2) is read as: the advisory fires when the *target* step (the one just navigated to) is empty — reassurance copy ("Step N has no entries yet — you can still work on it out of order"), not a warning about leaving an incomplete step behind.** | The sentence is genuinely ambiguous between "the step you're leaving is empty" and "the step you're jumping into is empty." The reassurance reading matches "gentle" far better than a warning tone, and is what the copy already drafted for this phase says. | OPEN |
| D-89 | 2026-08-02 | **P-05's read-only placeholder (an entry whose `methodId` this build doesn't recognize) is one component, `EntryRow`, branching on whether `getMethodById` resolved a plugin — not a separate `UnknownMethodEntryCard` file.** | The two cases share every action except Edit/Duplicate (reorder, delete, `a3Visibility` are identical regardless of `methodId`, per D-52); a second file would duplicate that shared 80% for a 20% visual/behavioral difference. | OPEN |
| D-90 | 2026-08-02 | **Ctrl/Cmd+Z and Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+Z are one global `window` keydown listener (`attachUndoRedoKeyboard`) routed to the project undo/redo stack — not scoped per text field.** | `SPEC.md` §4.3 says "undo/redo across the whole project"; a per-field handler would create two divergent undo notions (native browser text-undo vs. the project stack) when D-84 already establishes that the project stack is what text-field edits go through. A global handler is simpler and matches the spec's own framing. | OPEN |

### A second self-caught path-traversal bug in `history.rs` — found re-reading the same code that shipped it

| D-91 | 2026-08-02 | **`history_dir` (renamed `resolve_history_dir`, `src-tauri/src/ppsx/history.rs`) now validates `project_id` through the same single-path-segment check `timestamp` already used (`is_safe_path_component`: non-empty, not `.`/`..`, no `/`, `\`, NUL, or `:`) before joining it onto `app_local_data_dir`, and returns `Result` instead of an infallible `PathBuf`.** | `project_id` is `manifest.id` — read straight out of a `.ppsx` that can arrive by email (D-06) and never format-checked anywhere upstream (D-54 only checks manifest/project agree with *each other*, never that either value is safe as a path component). Unvalidated, a crafted `manifest.json` with `"id": "../../../../Library/LaunchAgents/evil"` — or a bare absolute path, which `PathBuf::join` accepts by silently discarding everything before it — would make `history_save` write the current project's content to an attacker-chosen location outside `app_local_data_dir` entirely, on the very first autosave tick after opening the file. Same failure shape as D-64/D-67 (a string from untrusted file content trusted as a path component instead of validated as one), one module over. Caught re-reading `history.rs` immediately after writing it, before any external review — not by one; recorded with its own D-number because it's the same class of near-miss D-67/D-68 caught by an independent pass in Phase 2, and this project's practice is to name a hole the moment it's found regardless of who found it, per the security-response protocol. | LOCKED |

### Post-implementation security review — independent Opus pass on `history.rs` + `archive.rs`'s Phase 3 additions, 2026-08-02

Requested for the same reason Phase 2 got one: `history.rs` is entirely new file-system-facing code built this phase, and `archive.rs` gained new write-path logic (D-77's compare-and-swap, D-78's caps) — both handle values that ultimately trace back to a `.ppsx` that can arrive from outside this machine (D-06). Scoped narrowly to those two files, not the frontend/state layer (already covered by the pre-implementation review behind D-69–D-84). Ran empirically — PoC corpora against real files, not just reading the code — and, per this project's practice, verified the self-caught D-91 fix rather than taking it on faith: confirmed it holds on macOS, then found the fix's blocklist had the exact host-OS-dependent gap D-67 had already fixed once in the zip-entry-name checker. Two more issues found and fixed the same way; two documented rather than fixed because closing them properly crosses the Rust/TypeScript IPC boundary or needs a primitive change to `atomic_write` that isn't justified by today's payload sizes — recorded as P-15/P-16 below. Full findings are in the review transcript, not reproduced here; D-92/D-93 record the fixes actually made.

| D-92 | 2026-08-02 | **`is_safe_path_component` (`history.rs`) changed from a blocklist (reject `/`, `\`, NUL, `:`, `.`, `..`) to an allowlist (`[A-Za-z0-9._-]`, ≤128 bytes, no all-dot name, no leading/trailing dot, not a Windows reserved device name).** | Empirically verified: the blocklist passed `".. "`, `"..."`, `"foo."` and `"CON"`/`"NUL"`/`"COM1"` — Windows strips trailing spaces/periods from a path component during Win32→NT normalization, so those resolve to the parent directory or a device despite failing a literal string-equality check, and reserved device names resolve to devices in any directory, with or without an extension. Same class of gap D-67 already closed once in `is_safe_relative_name` (host-OS-dependent path semantics), one module over. Bounded impact even unfixed — only one path component is joined, so the worst case was writing into `history/` or `app_local_data_dir/` itself, never outside it — but the allowlist costs nothing (the only legitimate inputs are a `crypto.randomUUID()` and `formatSnapshotTimestamp`'s output) and closes the whole class at once, including every Unicode homoglyph/zero-width/bidi-override trick tried against it. Verified with a 34-input hostile-`project_id` corpus run through the full `resolve_history_dir` → `write_snapshot` sequence and `canonicalize`d to confirm nothing escaped `app_local_data_dir`. | LOCKED |
| D-93 | 2026-08-02 | **`write_ppsx` (`archive.rs`) now rejects an `other_entries` name containing `\`, and rejects any entry name over 80 bytes (`MAX_ENTRY_NAME_BYTES`).** | Two independent write/read asymmetries, both the "write succeeds, read then permanently refuses" shape D-78 exists to close: (1) `write_ppsx` deduplicated entry names by exact string while `read_ppsx` dedupes *after* normalizing `\` to `/` (D-67) — so `assets/a.png` + `assets\a.png` wrote successfully and then made the file unopenable forever, empirically confirmed. (2) entry names were otherwise uncapped, and a zip name is billed against the read side's per-entry 256-byte header allowance twice (local header + central directory record) — enough very long names pass every write-side size cap and still exceed the read side's `file_size_ceiling`, measured at ~240 MB over budget for a constructed 2,000-entry/60,000-byte-name case. Neither is reachable by anything the app constructs today, which is exactly why it's worth closing now rather than after Phase 4 starts minting new `other_entries` names for images. | LOCKED |

## Phase 4 — A3 descriptor, HTML preview, xlsx export, `farplas-7step-tr`

Per `docs/00_KICKOFF.md`'s own model routing (D-28), this phase's architectural design
(the descriptor shape, the purity-boundary question, the fidelity-test methodology) was
done directly by this session (opus) rather than drafted-then-reviewed like Phases 2–3 —
there was no separate implementer session to hand a plan to. D-94–D-101 record the design
decisions; TEMPLATE_ANALYSIS.md §3/§9's already-verified geometry was transcribed into
`src/a3/templates/farplas-7step-tr.ts` as data, not re-derived.

| D-94 | 2026-08-02 | **The `src/domain`/`src/a3` ESLint purity boundary (D-43) is narrowed to exclude `src/a3/render/**`.** `HtmlA3Renderer.tsx` may import React; the rest of `src/a3` still may not. | `docs/01_ORIENTATION_REVIEW.md`'s own proposed layout already put `render/` (explicitly "React, CSS Grid") inside `src/a3/`, directly contradicting its own stated rule ("`src/a3/` imports nothing from React"). The invariant D-03/D-04 actually need is "no layout DECISIONS outside `buildA3Layout`," not "no React under `src/a3`" — `HtmlA3Renderer` maps an already-computed descriptor onto markup exactly the way the Rust `xlsx` writer maps it onto `rust_xlsxwriter` calls; neither may decide placement, both may use a UI-rendering library to serialize it. `buildA3Layout` itself, `descriptor.ts`, `methodContract.ts`, `templates/`, and `layout/` remain fully pure and covered by the boundary. | LOCKED |
| D-95 | 2026-08-02 | **Phase 4 builds only `farplas-7step-tr`.** `farplas-7step-plus`, `farplas-7step-en` and `pps-8step-auto` move to Phase 11. `farplas-7step-tr` remains `ProjectModel`'s default template through Phases 4–10 regardless of whether the fidelity test passes — D-10's "switch to `-plus` once the fidelity test passes" trigger is deferred until `-plus` is actually built. `SPEC.md` §6 Phase 11's done-condition is corrected from "switching a project between all three templates" to "all four." Closes P-03, P-04. | The user's Phase 4 task list named exactly one template file (`src/a3/templates/farplas-7step-tr.ts`); building all four would roughly quadruple this phase's template-authoring work for three templates nothing yet exercises (Template B's geometry is still separately blocked per D-27/P-19-shaped work, and `-plus`/`-en` are trivial variations once `-tr`'s pipeline is proven). Budgeting one phase's work to one phase, per Anayasa Madde 1. | LOCKED |
| D-96 | 2026-08-02 | **`ProjectModel.signOff` is not exported into the A3 sheet's footer yet.** `TR_FOOTER_FIELDS`' three approval cells (`ONAY 1` / `ONAY 2` / `MÜD-YÖN. ONAYI`) are label-only — `resolveFooterFieldValue` always returns blank. | Verified against `TEMPLATE_ANALYSIS.md` §4/§9.1: none of the three approval cells has a distinct value cell in the source form — on the printed sheet the label *is* the whole cell, and sign-off is a wet-ink signature next to it, not typed data. Forcing `signOff.preparedBy?.name` etc. into that same cell would silently overwrite the printed label. See P-18 for what a real binding would need. | LOCKED |
| D-97 | 2026-08-02 | **P-08's "byte-level regression is unachievable" is resolved as a normalized structural comparison, not a visual PDF-diff against the original `.xls`.** `src-tauri/tests/xlsx.rs` writes a real, checked-in descriptor fixture through `write_a3_workbook`, then reads the result back two ways: `calamine` for cell values and merged-range counts, and direct zip/XML inspection (`xl/worksheets/sheet1.xml`, `xl/styles.xml`) for paper size, orientation, fit-to-page, margins, fonts and PDCA fill colors. The descriptor fixture (`src-tauri/tests/fixtures/a3-layout-descriptor.json`) is generated by `scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` — the real `buildA3Layout` output, never hand-written — the same D-62 philosophy as the `.ppsx` fixture corpus, run via `npx vite-node scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` whenever the descriptor shape or the template changes. Closes P-08. | SPEC.md §6's literal ask (render both the export and `PPS_A3_Format_TR.xls` to PDF and diff images) needs a LibreOffice-headless render pipeline this environment cannot run reliably or deterministically in CI — and P-08 already flagged the byte-level alternative as broken (creation timestamps, zip-ordering drift across `rust_xlsxwriter` versions). A structural comparison is the right *automated* gate; the true pixel-identity claim in SPEC.md §6 still needs a one-time manual check (open the exported file next to the original in Excel) before Phase 4 is declared visually done — not something this session can perform from a sandboxed shell with no Excel/LibreOffice available. | OPEN — revisit if LibreOffice-headless PDF rendering becomes available in CI |
| D-98 | 2026-08-02 | **Export performance budget: ≤5 s for up to 100 embedded images/drawing objects, measured starting from `write_a3_workbook` receiving an already-built descriptor.** Frontend chart rendering and PNG encoding happen before the Tauri `invoke` and are explicitly outside this budget. Closes P-13. | `SPEC.md` §3.3's "under 3 s for 20 images" is superseded — two of the five real company A3s carry 83 and 92 drawing objects (`docs/02_REAL_WORLD_FINDINGS.md` §2.5). 100 gives headroom over the observed maximum; 5 s is a judgment call for a one-click, user-initiated export (not a background operation), not a value derived from a benchmark run in this session — no perf test ships this phase, and P-18-adjacent full-scale benchmarking against real image counts is flagged as a follow-up rather than silently skipped. | OPEN — revisit once a real perf benchmark exists |
| D-99 | 2026-08-02 | **`src/a3/methodContract.ts` is a new, pure seam between `buildA3Layout` and `src/methods`'s React-bearing `MethodPlugin` registry.** `MethodPlugin<TPayload>` (`src/methods/types.ts`) gained `renderToA3`, the field D-07 always specified but Phase 3 explicitly deferred; `ErasedMethodPlugin`'s existing type-erasure pattern (D-82) extended to cover it. `buildA3Layout` never imports `src/methods` — it receives an `A3EntryRendererMap` built by `getA3RendererMap()` (`src/methods/registry.ts`) from its caller (`src/app/routes/workspace/a3Preview.ts`, the composition root). | `MethodPlugin` imports `react` (for `Editor`), which `src/a3` may never import (D-43) even with D-94's carve-out (only `render/` is exempt, and `buildA3Layout` itself is not). Injecting the renderer map as a parameter — rather than `buildA3Layout` reaching into `src/methods` — keeps the pure function testable with a two-line fake map (see `buildA3Layout.test.ts`) and keeps D-07's promise intact: adding a method is still one plugin file plus one registry-array entry, never a second registry to maintain in sync. | LOCKED |
| D-100 | 2026-08-02 | **Entries place one wrapped text line per content row (D-40's 19 pt authored floor assumes exactly this), and an entry is placed only if *all* of its lines fit the block's remaining budget — never split across primary/appendix.** Entries that don't fit are dropped from the A3 sheet but always re-appear in an appendix sheet, whether or not the user marked them `appendix` — `buildAppendixSheets` unions `a3Visibility === 'appendix'` with every `droppedEntryIds` id across all blocks. | SPEC.md §2.3: "the export must never silently truncate content." Splitting a single entry's lines across primary and appendix would produce a document where half a thought is on the sheet and half is three tabs away — worse than moving the whole entry. The union with `droppedEntryIds` is what makes budget overflow *safe by construction* rather than merely detected — an entry that overflows behaves exactly like one the user already chose to route to an appendix, not a new failure mode the UI has to handle specially. | LOCKED |
| D-101 | 2026-08-02 | **Excel column-width→point conversion is an approximation confined to `HtmlA3Renderer`'s screen/print preview; the Rust `xlsx` writer never converts — it passes each column's native character-width unit to `rust_xlsxwriter::set_column_width` verbatim.** The conversion formula (`excelColumnWidthToPt`, `src/a3/layout/measure.ts`) is the same `px = round(charWidth × 7 + 5)` widely used by tools like openpyxl (Calibri-11 metrics), not a value Excel guarantees for every installed font. | D-04's "Rust is a dumb serializer" is only actually true if Rust never performs a lossy unit conversion of its own — `set_column_width` already accepts the same character-width unit our descriptor stores, so passing it straight through is the zero-conversion path. The preview's pixel width is therefore a best-effort visual approximation, not a value the export depends on; a preview/export mismatch here is a rendering nicety gap, never a WYSIWYG-guarantee violation, because the export never reads the approximated value at all. | LOCKED |

## Phase 5 — method plugins wave 1

Two open design questions were raised before any implementation (per `CLAUDE.md`'s
"before writing code for a new area, write a short plan and let me approve it") and
presented to Barış with a recommended answer and two alternatives each, rather than
silently picked — D-102 and D-103 record what was approved. Pareto and Fishbone were
then built as the two reference implementations proving the pattern before the
remaining six methods were built against it, per D-28's own Opus/Sonnet routing
(this session ran entirely on Sonnet 5 — see the note under D-102).

| D-102 | 2026-08-02 | **A method contributes a chart/diagram to its block as a pure spec, never pixels.** `A3BlockContent` (`src/a3/methodContract.ts`) gains `image?: A3ImageRequest` (a chart/diagram consuming `rowSpan` content rows in the existing vertical text-line stack) and `zones?: readonly A3ContentZone[]` (a horizontal partition of one reserved row-span into text/image sub-regions — generic, not hardcoded to any step; SMART Target is simply the first user). `place.ts`/`placeZones.ts` compute geometry only (anchor cell, width/height in pt, from the block's real per-row heights — `bodyRowHeightPt` alone is wrong wherever a block's rows aren't uniform, e.g. Step 2's block ends on a 93.75 pt row). `buildA3Layout`'s return type changes from `A3LayoutDescriptor` to `{ descriptor, pendingImages }` — `pendingImages` is discovered in the *same* block-iteration pass that produces the descriptor, so geometry can never disagree between the two outputs. The composition root (`a3Preview.ts`) calls `buildA3Layout` once with no images, rasterizes each pending slot's spec to PNG off-screen (new `src/a3/render/rasterize.ts`, a D-94-style purity-boundary carve-out, using `html-to-image` — works uniformly for Recharts' pure-SVG output and React Flow's mixed DOM/SVG output, one capture path not two), then calls `buildA3Layout` again with the bytes in `options.images` to get the final, fully-baked descriptor both `HtmlA3Renderer` and the Rust writer consume unchanged. `RightPanel.tsx`'s descriptor build moved from a synchronous `useMemo` to an effect with a `cancelled` guard, since the second pass is genuinely async. Which React node renders a given `A3ImageKind` is dependency-injected the same way `renderToA3` already is (D-99): `MethodPlugin` gained optional `imageKind`/`renderImage` fields, and `src/methods/registry.ts`'s new `getA3ImageRendererMap()` assembles the map `rasterize.ts` receives as a parameter — `src/a3` still never imports `src/methods`. | Three options were on the table: (1) this two-call shape; (2) making `ImagePlacement.data` optional and mutating the descriptor after one `buildA3Layout` call, which would have widened the export-facing/IPC-crossing type with a "pending" state that only discipline (not the type system) keeps from leaking into Rust or the preview; (3) a live Recharts/React-Flow component in `HtmlA3Renderer` for screen preview with PNG baking only at export time, which is cheaper per keystroke but makes `HtmlA3Renderer` a second place that must understand `ChartSpec`/graph shapes, contradicting D-94's "dumb serializer" framing and reopening exactly the preview/export divergence risk D-03/D-05 exist to close. Barış approved (1). This does touch already-shipped Phase 4 code (`buildA3Layout`'s signature, its one call site, `buildA3Layout.test.ts`, `HtmlA3Renderer.test.tsx`, `scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts`) — flagged rather than silently done, per `CLAUDE.md`. Known gap, not solved this phase: an image-bearing entry that overflows to an appendix sheet currently loses its image there (appendix sheets only ever carried text) — same class of gap as Phase 4's un-ingested `Entry.images` (P-18). Model-routing note: the task's own routing asked for Opus on this design work plus the Pareto/Fishbone reference implementations, then Sonnet for the rest (mirroring D-28) — this session had no way to switch models mid-conversation and, given the choice, Barış opted to proceed entirely on Sonnet 5 rather than pause to switch, so all eight Phase 5 methods were designed and built on Sonnet 5. | LOCKED |
| D-103 | 2026-08-02 | **Fishbone's payload stores a fixed, algorithmically-positioned category spine plus freeform cause nodes — not a fully freeform React Flow graph, and not a positionless semantic list either.** `categorySet` selects one of `4M`/`5M1E`/`6M`/`8P` (D-11: Step 4 only); each category's position is computed from the set, never persisted or draggable. Each cause carries `{ id, categoryId, parentCauseId?, text, position? }` — React-Flow-shaped (`id`/`position` match `Node<T>`), one level of sub-cause nesting via `parentCauseId`. Edges are *derived* from `categoryId`/`parentCauseId` at render/editor-mount time, never separately persisted — storing both a parent reference and an independent edge list is exactly the "two representations of one relationship, no precedence rule" bug class D-71 already paid for once (`Entry.order` vs. array position). Renders to its A3 block via D-102's `image: { kind: "fishbone-diagram" }` mechanism, mounting the same node/edge structure off-screen that the editor shows. | The alternative of a fully freeform graph (categories draggable too) was rejected as more editor complexity this phase for no Phase-5 payoff, and it would leave no canonical "this is a fishbone" structure for Phase 7's future gate rules to validate against. The alternative of no positions at all (always auto-layout, editor and export both compute placement) was rejected because it doesn't honor SPEC.md §8.8's "the user can drag, rename and extend it like any hand-built diagram" — a cluttered diagram could never be nudged. Barış approved the fixed-spine/freeform-cause middle ground. | LOCKED |
| D-104 | 2026-08-02 | **Fishbone's 6M category set is 5M1E's six categories (Man, Machine, Material, Method, Measurement, Environment) plus a distinct seventh-in-practice "Management" category; 8P is Price, Product, Place, Promotion, People, Process, Physical Evidence, Productivity.** | Neither set is defined in `SPEC.md` (only 4M and 5M1E are). Two conventions exist for 6M in practice — some shops use it as a bare synonym for 5M1E's own six categories under a different name, others add a distinct Management axis — and guessing wrong would have baked incorrect domain terminology into locked TR/EN i18n keys for an audience (SPEC.md: "quality/process engineers... they know the terminology") that would notice. Barış confirmed the Management-axis convention and the standard service-industry 8P set rather than the tool silently picking one. | LOCKED |

### Post-implementation architecture review — independent Opus pass on D-102's pipeline, 2026-08-03

Requested by Barış before moving to Phase 6, for the same reason Phases 2 and 3 each got one:
D-102 introduced an entirely new mechanism (a chart/diagram rasterization pipeline), changed
the signature of already-shipped Phase 4 code (`buildA3Layout`), and was designed and built
end-to-end on Sonnet 5 rather than the Opus the D-28 routing asks for on architecture. Scoped
to `methodContract.ts`, `place.ts`, `placeZones.ts`, `buildA3Layout.ts`, `rasterize.ts`,
`registry.ts` and the four chart/diagram components. Run empirically — probe tests against the
real functions, not code reading — per this project's established practice (D-67, D-91, D-92).
Four defects found, all four confirmed by reproduction before any fix; D-105–D-108 record them.
The most serious (D-105) would have shipped a feature that passes every test and is broken in
every real run, which is exactly the "doğru ama görünmez" blind spot the Anayasa's §3b table
names — worth stating plainly rather than filing quietly.

| D-105 | 2026-08-03 | **`rasterize.ts` captured the off-screen host before React had committed to it, so every exported chart and diagram would have been a blank PNG.** Fixed by wrapping the mount in `flushSync`, adding a `settleLayout()` frame yield for measurement-driven libraries, and — the structural half — giving every chart/diagram an **explicit pixel box** (`A3ImageSize`, passed to `renderImage(spec, size)`) instead of Recharts' `ResponsiveContainer` / inherited layout. Every Recharts series also gained `isAnimationActive={false}`. | `createRoot(host).render(node)` is asynchronous: measured directly, the host's `innerHTML` is still `""` immediately after `render()` **and** after one microtask, only populating after a macrotask. The original code called `toPng(host)` on the next statement. Reproduced end to end: with the fix removed, the new regression test fails with `expected '' to contain 'CHART'` — the literal blank capture. Three independent aggravating factors made this worse than a race: `ResponsiveContainer` renders nothing until a ResizeObserver cycle completes (guaranteed to be pending in a freshly-mounted off-screen div), React Flow's `fitView` needs the same measurement, and Recharts animates series from zero on mount so even a late capture could catch bars mid-flight. Invisible to the test suite by construction — `rasterize.test.ts` mocks `html-to-image`, so no assertion could ever have observed the empty host. Anayasa §3b's "çalışma-zamanı davranışı kaynak kodda görünmez" row, and the reason this review was worth its cost. | LOCKED |
| D-106 | 2026-08-03 | **An image-bearing entry whose text exactly filled its block was reported as *placed* while emitting an image slot with `heightPt: 0`, anchored one row past the block's own last row.** `place.ts` now treats "the image has no room" (`imageRowSpan <= 0`) as "the entry does not fit" and routes the whole entry to an appendix, per D-100. | Reproduced: a 2-row block with a renderer emitting 2 text lines plus a `rowSpan`-less image produced `placedEntryIds: ['e1']` and `pendingImages: [{ anchorCell: 'B3', heightPt: 0 }]` — outside a block spanning rows 1–2. Downstream that is a zero-height canvas handed to the rasterizer and a degenerate image embedded at a cell belonging to no block. Reachable today via Fishbone, whose `renderToA3` omits `rowSpan` deliberately: any Step 4 state leaving exactly as many rows as its title wraps to hits it. The old guard only rejected `imageRowSpan < 0`, so exactly-zero passed. | LOCKED |
| D-107 | 2026-08-03 | **A `zones` entry with more zones than the block has content columns silently dropped the surplus zones' text and charts** — no dropped id, no overflow warning, no trace. `placeZonesContent` now refuses the whole entry (returns `undefined`) unless every zone gets at least one column, sending it to an appendix instead. | Reproduced: four zones into a two-column block placed zones A and B and silently discarded zone C's text and zone D's chart, while reporting the entry as successfully placed. This is precisely the silent truncation SPEC.md §2.3 forbids and D-100 exists to make impossible — and it was in the *generic* zone mechanism, not in SMART Target's particular use of it, so it would have been inherited by every future zones-based method. Not reachable through today's only caller (Step 3 offers 14 columns for 3 zones), which is exactly why it needed finding before a second caller arrives. | LOCKED |
| D-108 | 2026-08-03 | **`buildAppendixSheets` read only `content.lines`, so an entry whose content lives entirely in `zones` appendixed as a completely blank sheet.** A new `flattenContentForAppendix` folds zone text into the appendix in reading order and records each chart/diagram as a `[kind]` placeholder line. Supersedes the "documented, not fixed" half of P-20. | For SMART Target this was a total loss, not a degraded one: `renderSmartTargetToA3` returns `lines: []` by design (all its content is zoned), so a dropped or user-appendixed SMART Target produced an appendix sheet containing nothing at all — not even its title. SMART Target is the one method D-12/D-33 make *mandatory*, and the appendix is the mechanism SPEC.md §2.3 relies on to promise nothing is ever lost; a silently empty appendix for the mandatory step is the worst possible place for this bug. Appendix sheets still carry no image placements of their own — that residual gap stays open as P-20. | LOCKED |
| D-109 | 2026-08-03 | **`rasterizePendingImages` uses `Promise.allSettled`, not `Promise.all` — one failing chart no longer costs the user every other chart and the entire A3 preview.** | With `Promise.all`, a single rejected rasterization (a malformed spec, a font fetch blocked by the Tauri CSP per D-14's locked-down policy) rejected the whole `buildProjectA3Layout` call, so `RightPanel` fell back to "the A3 preview could not be built" — the user loses the complete document because one chart failed. Per-image isolation degrades to "that block shows its text, without its chart," which is both recoverable and honest. | LOCKED |
