## Phase 6 entry gates — what running the unrun paths actually found, 2026-08-03

Phase 6 opened with two gates rather than with scope: P-19 (`cargo` had never run in
any session) and P-21 (no chart had ever been rasterized in a real browser). Both were
"yürütülmemiş yol" items in the Anayasa §3b sense — not code anyone could read a defect
out of. Both gates went red on first execution, and both defects were in code every
existing test called green. Recorded here rather than folded quietly into Phase 6's
implementation, per this project's practice of naming a hole the moment it is found.

| D-110 | 2026-08-03 | **P-19 was never a missing toolchain — it was a missing `PATH` entry.** The Rust toolchain is fully installed at `~/.rustup/toolchains/stable-aarch64-apple-darwin/bin`; Homebrew's `rustup` formula never created `~/.cargo/bin` and never linked shims into `/opt/homebrew/bin`, so `cargo`/`rustc` resolve to nothing in a default shell while `rustup toolchain list` happily reports an active `stable`. Prepending that directory to `PATH` makes all three commands work. Closes P-19. | Phase 5 recorded "this environment has no Rust toolchain at all (`cargo`/`rustc` not found, no `~/.cargo`)" and treated it as a hard environment gap — a correct observation with a wrong conclusion, because the check stopped at `which cargo`. The distinction matters beyond convenience: the "gap" framing meant Phase 5's Rust diff shipped *verified by reading*, and reading is exactly what missed D-111 below. An environment probe that returns "absent" deserves one more question — absent, or unreachable? | LOCKED |
| D-111 | 2026-08-03 | **A static template merge and a D-102 `zones` merge could claim the same cells, and `rust_xlsxwriter` rejects the overlap — 7 of 9 xlsx tests failed the first time `cargo test` ever ran.** `buildA3Layout` now partitions merges: any `template.merges` entry geometrically overlapping a dynamically-placed one is dropped in favour of the dynamic one (`rangesOverlap`, built on the existing `cellRef.ts` parsers). | `farplas-7step-tr.ts` carries `B58:O58` — a whole-row merge for Step 3's single content row, correct geometry for an empty block or a plain `lines` entry, and written in Phase 4 when those were the only two cases. Phase 5's SMART Target then split that same row into `B58:F58`/`M58:O58` via `zones`, and `write_a3_workbook` refused the workbook outright (`MergeRangeOverlaps`). Invisible to the entire TypeScript suite by construction: the descriptor is *data*, and nothing on the TS side knows that overlapping merges are illegal — only the actual Excel writer does, and it had never been run against a zoned descriptor. The fix is deliberately generic rather than special-casing Step 3: every future zoned or otherwise sub-divided block hits the identical static-vs-dynamic collision, and the second occurrence should not need a second diagnosis. | LOCKED |
| D-112 | 2026-08-03 | **`scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` gives every embedded image distinct pixel bytes instead of reusing one placeholder PNG.** A small CRC32/deflate PNG encoder in the script emits a 1×1 image in a per-slot colour. | With one byte-identical placeholder across all five slots, `rust_xlsxwriter` deduplicated them into a single `xl/media/image1.png` referenced by five drawing anchors — correct OOXML behaviour, and precisely the behaviour that made `embedded_images_land_in_the_workbook_as_real_media` (strengthened in Phase 5 to an exact per-image media count) fail at 1 vs 5. The test was right and the fixture was unrealistic: real rasterized charts never share bytes. Loosening the assertion would have thrown away the only automated proof that N descriptor images survive as N distinct media files — D-62's fixture philosophy says fix the fixture, not the expectation. | LOCKED |
| D-113 | 2026-08-03 | **`rasterize.ts` mounts its capture host at on-screen coordinates (`top: 0; left: 0`) inside a zero-size `overflow: hidden` wrapper, never at a large negative offset.** The previous `-10000px` host captured as a uniformly white PNG in a real browser even with D-105's `flushSync` + `settleLayout` fixes in place. Closes P-21. | Walked in a real Chromium, which is the only thing that could have found it: jsdom has no paint and every unit test mocks `html-to-image`. An offset sweep measured the behaviour directly — at `0px` the capture carries full content (462 distinct colours, 22 % non-white), at `-100px` it is already partially blank (204 colours, 13 %), and at `-1000px` and `-10000px` it is uniformly white (1 colour, 0 % non-white). The React tree was confirmed committed and painting real SVG at every offset, so this is viewport culling at rasterization time, not a React timing issue — a *third* hazard beneath D-105's two, and one D-105's fix could never have reached. After the change all four real chart/diagram kinds (Pareto, Trend, Trajectory, Fishbone) produce correctly-sized, non-blank, mutually distinct PNGs through the real `rasterizePendingImage`. The lesson is D-105's restated with the emphasis moved: D-105 proved the tree was *committed*; only running it proved anything was *painted*, and "committed" was never the same claim. | LOCKED |

## Phase 6 — method plugins wave 2

Scope and the three new-subsystem questions were settled with Barış before any
implementation, the same practice as Phases 2, 3 and 5. Counting §1.3 line by line
against what Phase 5 shipped gives **47 remaining methods** — roughly six times Phase 5's
eight — of which 5 need image ingestion and 5 need cross-step references. Step 3 turned
out to have **zero** remaining: the SMART card, the baseline→target chart (D-38's zone B)
and the stakeholder note all closed in Phase 5.

| D-114 | 2026-08-03 | **Phase 6 ships as five slices, not one phase.** 6a: shared row-table substrate + Steps 1–2's ten methods that need no image, no cross-step reference and no new chart type. 6b: the cross-step reference subsystem + the five reference-bearing methods + Step 4's five plain ones. 6c: Steps 5–6 plain + Step 2's distribution charts (histogram/scatter/box, which extend `ChartSpec`). 6d: Steps 7–8 plain, including the `rounds` (D-58) and `signOff` bindings. 6e: image ingestion + annotation + the five image-bearing methods. Barış approved the map and authorised **6a only**. | `SPEC.md` §6 defines Phase 6 as "everything remaining in §1.3", which is 47 methods and at least three new subsystems — attempting it in one phase is Anayasa G1 (unbudgeted large work) and G4 (bloated session) simultaneously. Five slices is more than the three the split was sketched with, and that is the point: D-95 set the precedent by cutting Phase 4 from four templates to one rather than reporting a tidier number. Constraint driving the boundaries: **at most one new subsystem per slice**, so a slice never debugs two unproven mechanisms at once — the failure shape D-105 and D-113 both came out of. 6e is last because it is the riskiest (untrusted image bytes from an emailed `.ppsx`, the D-64/D-67/D-91/D-92 class) and because nothing else depends on it; flagged at approval time that it can be pulled forward if photo support is needed for a Farplas demo, since real company A3s are photo-heavy (`docs/02_REAL_WORLD_FINDINGS.md`). | LOCKED |
| D-115 | 2026-08-03 | **6a extracts `src/methods/shared/rowTable.ts` + `RowTableEditor.tsx` up front, before the second repetition rather than after it.** At least five of 6a's ten methods are a "row list + owner + date" table, and most of the 37 plain methods still to come are the same shape. | Anayasa Madde 2 says abstract the moment repetition is *seen*, not once it has been paid for twice; `whyChain.ts`/`WhyChainEditor.tsx` (Phase 5, shared by `fiveWhy` and `threeLeggedFiveWhy`) is the in-repo precedent that this pays off. Doing it up front is the one thing that makes the remaining slices cheaper rather than merely longer, and it is why 6a can hold ten methods where Phase 5 held eight. | LOCKED |
| D-116 | 2026-08-03 | **Cross-step references are a first-class field on `Entry` — `references?: { role: string; targetEntryId: string }[]` — not three named fields and not anything inside `payload`.** `role` is a loose string with documented constants (`pointOfCause`, `rootCause`, `countermeasure`), not an enum. Ships in 6b; nothing in 6a holds a reference. | `SPEC.md` §4.2 writes `countermeasure.rootCauseIds[]`, `rootCause.pointOfCauseId` and `action.countermeasureId`, but that sketch describes *semantics*, not a field count — the same way §4.2 sketched `readiness` inside `StepState` (D-53 derived it instead) and §4.1 put `history/` in the zip (D-74 sidecarred it). Inside `payload` is the option that actually breaks: D-52 makes payload opaque and never inspected at the model level, so no generic code could see a reference at all — orphan detection, Phase 7's traceability view and any warn-before-delete would each need to know every method's payload shape, which is the concrete mechanism by which "half of wave 2 gets rewritten". Three named fields on `Entry` avoid that but buy three code paths in every generic consumer and a schema change per new relation type; one `role`-discriminated list keeps D-51's loose-by-default posture, so a later method can introduce a relation without a migration. | LOCKED |
| D-117 | 2026-08-03 | **Deleting a referenced entry is permitted, changes nothing on the referring entries, and leaves the reference dangling.** Orphans are surfaced by a derived selector (`findOrphanedReferences`, `src/domain/selectors/`), never stored; the warning UI is Phase 7's, the selector and the data ship in 6b. Referential integrity is *not* checked at load — a `.ppsx` with a dangling reference must still open. | `SPEC.md` §4.2 says the app "warns on orphans", not that it prevents them. Cascade-delete would destroy a countermeasure because someone deleted a root cause, on a document whose entire purpose is to be defensible in an IATF audit; refusing the delete fights the user for a condition the spec calls a warning. Not mutating referrers also makes undo correct for free: entry deletion is already an undoable command (D-70), so undo restores the target and every dangling reference resolves again with no compensating logic — had deletion rewritten the referrers, undo would have had to reverse that too. Deriving rather than storing is D-53's rule applied one layer over: a stored integrity verdict goes stale the moment an entry moves, and the file would then disagree with the app. Validating at load would additionally break D-59's read-only-open promise for files from newer builds. | LOCKED |
| D-118 | 2026-08-03 | **Image ingestion is owned end-to-end by Rust; the webview never sees raw file bytes.** A Tauri dialog returns a path; Rust reads the file, applies EXIF orientation to the pixels and then **strips EXIF unconditionally**, downscales, generates a thumbnail (`image` crate, SPEC §5), and writes both into the `.ppsx` immediately through the same `expected_modified_ms` compare-and-swap path `ppsx_write` uses (D-77). The frontend receives an `ImageRef` plus a small thumbnail. **An image that came out of a `.ppsx` is never re-decoded in Rust** — the stored thumbnail is handed to the webview as opaque bytes for its own decoder. On the A3, `A3ImageRequest` gains a source discriminant (`spec`-sourced = chart, must be rasterized; `asset`-sourced = photo, bytes already exist); `place.ts` computes identical geometry for both and only the composition root's resolver forks. Architectural half decided now; numeric caps and the exact IPC surface land with 6e. | Four separate calls, one rationale each. **Rust owns the bytes** because this is the moment D-81 explicitly deferred to ("revisit when Phase 4/5 add image or chart assets") — a 5 MB photo couriered as a JSON number array across the IPC bridge is the cost that decision was waiting for. **Immediate container write** rather than a sidecar keeps one storage location and inherits D-56's crash safety and D-77's conflict safety for free, at the price of rewriting the zip per photo added; a sidecar would need its own crash story for assets staged but not yet saved. **EXIF always, not just before a provider call** because `CLAUDE.md` scopes stripping to the AI path, but D-06 makes a `.ppsx` emailable — plant-floor GPS coordinates travel with the file whether or not AI is ever enabled, and one unconditional strip point cannot be forgotten by a later phase. **Never re-decoding a `.ppsx` image in Rust** is the security call: generating thumbnails on open would mean parsing attacker-controlled image data in a process with full filesystem access, which is the D-64/D-67/D-91/D-92 failure class with an image decoder in place of a path parser; the webview's decoder is sandboxed and hardened, Rust's is not. Reusing D-102's *geometry* but not its *rasterize round-trip* follows from the mechanism's own logic — `pendingImages` exists because a chart has no bytes until something draws it, which is not true of a photograph. Closes the `Entry.images` half of P-18. | LOCKED |
| D-119 | 2026-08-03 | **Annotation is scoped to images in Phase 6 and stored as structured data, never baked into stored pixels.** `ImageRef` gains `annotations?: Annotation[]` in normalized (0..1) coordinates. At export an annotated image routes through the existing D-102 rasterize path as a `spec`-sourced slot whose renderer draws the photo plus an SVG overlay; an unannotated one takes D-118's direct `asset` path. D-42's "available on every block, not only the Step 1 photo board" half is **deferred with a reason, not dropped**. | `SPEC.md` §1.3 names annotation on exactly the image methods (photo board, spaghetti, VSM, before/after), while D-42 widens it to every block. Honouring D-42 literally in Phase 6 means a free-canvas overlay across the whole A3 that no template geometry describes — a slice-sized feature on its own, next to 47 methods. Normalized coordinates are what let one annotation survive the image being scaled differently per block or per template. Compositing only at render time keeps the stored form editable, re-renderable and machine-readable, so this is not the pasted-screenshot artifact D-39 rejects — D-39 is about content structure, and the structure stays. Compositing in the frontend rather than emitting xlsx drawing shapes keeps D-04 intact (Rust would otherwise need shape geometry logic) and costs **zero** new export machinery, since it reuses the capture path charts already use — including D-113's fix. Flagged for 6e: `RASTER_SCALE = 3` is a global constant, and recapturing an already-full-resolution photo at 3× would produce an absurdly large PNG, so the scale likely needs to become per-slot. | LOCKED |

## Phase 6a — Steps 1–2's ten plain methods, 2026-08-03

DONE. Ten methods against D-114's 6a scope, following Phase 5's exact file
shape (`index.ts`/`schema.ts`/`Editor.tsx`/`renderToA3.ts` + a schema test, an
Editor test and a renderToA3 test each) — Gap Statement, 5W2H, Problem type
classifier, TPM loss taxonomy, VOC/complaint record, Containment/ICA (Step
1); Stratification matrix, Check sheet, Process flow/SIPOC, MSA/Gage R&R note
(Step 2). No new subsystem: every method is payload → schema → Editor →
`renderToA3` text lines, the same pattern Phase 5 established, plus one
abstraction (D-115) pulled forward one repetition early. `references[]`
(D-116), image ingestion (D-118) and the distribution-chart `ChartSpec`
extension are untouched, exactly as scoped.

| D-120 | 2026-08-03 | **Every field in a row-table row (`rowTable.ts`, D-115) is a plain `string` — including PPM, tally counts and dates.** No per-column numeric or `Date` typing. | These fields are recorded, never computed on (nothing sums a PPM column or sorts a date column yet), so a numeric/date type would buy nothing beyond D-51's loose `z.string()` default while adding per-field type branching to one shared editor and formatter that has to stay generic across five different row shapes. `<input type="date">` already round-trips a plain ISO string with no parsing on either side. Revisit only if a future method needs to aggregate a row-table column (a real Check Sheet total, say) — that method can layer its own numeric parsing over the still-string-typed field without changing the shared substrate. | LOCKED |
| D-121 | 2026-08-03 | **The stratification matrix gets a ninth `count` column beyond `SPEC.md` §1.3's eight named dimensions (line/shift/machine/cavity/operator/supplier/date/product).** | A stratification matrix that only classifies occurrences by stratum, with nothing recording how many occurrences fall in each stratum, cannot show which stratum accounts for most of the problem — the entire reason to stratify in the first place. `SPEC.md` describes the dimensions, not the full field list, the same gap D-38/D-98 already filled elsewhere by adding an implied-but-unstated field. | LOCKED |
| D-122 | 2026-08-03 | **TPM loss taxonomy (D-110's §3.0 category set) is a fixed seven-row grid — each category a `{ applies: boolean, severity }` tag — not a row table.** Mirrors `isIsNot`'s fixed-dimension shape (Phase 5), not D-115's variable-length row list. | The seven categories are a closed domain taxonomy the company already reports against, not a user-extensible list — nothing about the tool calls for adding an eighth category or removing "Cost". A row table would let a user rename or delete a company-standard category by accident; a fixed grid can't. | LOCKED |

DONE. D-114's second slice: the `Entry.references[]` subsystem (D-116/D-117)
plus the five reference-bearing methods and Step 4's five plain ones. Scope

actually shipped rather than from the slice sketch — which is what surfaced
D-123. Two design questions were put to Barış before any code was written
(D-123, D-124); both were answered, and both answers are what the
implementation follows.

| D-123 | 2026-08-04 | **The Step 4 PFMEA linkage is a *plain* method, and the fifth reference-bearing method is Step 6's action plan.** `SPEC.md` §1.3 describes PFMEA linkage with the word "reference", but a PFMEA is an external controlled document, and §4.2 already houses those in `meta.linkedRecords[]` (`'8D' \| 'NCR' \| 'CAR' \| 'PFMEA' \| 'ControlPlan'`) at project level. D-116's `Entry.references[]` addresses *entries*. So 6b = POC nomination, hypothesis verification table, countermeasure, action item, ICA→PCA tracker (reference-bearing) + Why-Why tree, fault tree, X-Y matrix, PFMEA linkage, comparative analysis (plain). Barış chose this reading over deferring the action plan to 6c. | This is the only reading under which **both** of D-114's own counts hold: "the five reference-bearing methods" *and* "Step 4's five plain ones". Counting PFMEA linkage as reference-bearing leaves Step 4 with four plain methods and leaves §4.2's third relation ("an action holds `countermeasureId`") unimplemented in the slice that builds the mechanism for it — the reference subsystem would ship with one of the three relations it exists to express having no method that uses it. | LOCKED |
| D-124 | 2026-08-04 | **One traceable node = one `Entry`.** The countermeasure "list", the action plan "table" and the ICA→PCA tracker are one entry per record, not one entry holding a row table. The step's entries band *is* the list `SPEC.md` §1.3 names. | Forced by D-116: references live on the `Entry`, so an entry holding N countermeasures could carry only the *union* of their root-cause links — and §1.2 S5 ("flag if **any** countermeasure is not linked to at least one verified root cause") is a per-countermeasure rule a union cannot answer. Same for S6 per action. The alternative considered and rejected was extending the reference shape with a `sourceRowId`: generic code would still see the link, but the chain node becomes an `(entryId, rowId)` pair that Phase 7's traceability view could only *label* by reading the payload — reintroducing exactly the payload-awareness D-116 exists to prevent. | D-125 | 2026-08-04 | **The reference picker is generic shell UI, driven by a `MethodPlugin.referenceRoles` declaration; no method's `Editor` ever touches references.** `EntryEditorDialog` renders one `EntryReferenceField` per declared role beside the title field. A role is `{ role, labelKey, emptyKey, fromSteps, multiple }`. Picking dispatches `entry.update` **directly**, not through D-84's ~600 ms coalescing. | `MethodEditorProps` is `{ payload, onChange }` and references sit *outside* `payload` — so a plugin-rendered picker would need a second write channel into `Entry`, which is the coupling D-116 chose the field position to avoid. Revisit if a future real-webview walkthrough still drops an image after this — that would rule out concurrency and point back at a chart-kind-specific WebKit incompatibility instead. | LOCKED |

In rows: 15/24/11 and 19/8/8/8/7 of 50. | Barış chose this over literal fidelity (option A) and over "a visual in every step" (option C), each presented with its own capacity table. §11.3 flagged exactly two numbers as measurable problems — ADIM 2's over-allocation (the same shape §6 criticised in Template A at 65.4 %) and ADIM 8's four unstructured rows (§6 item 7) — and this corrects those two while leaving Rev00's skeleton intact everywhere else, so the template stays a transcription with two named, justified deviations rather than a redesign. Option A left ADIM 3 with 5 canvas rows (no complete entry fits) and ADIM 8 with 3; option C bought a fourth chart-capable block by moving ADIM 2 from 60 % to 36 %, the largest departure from D-150's "build on this". | **SUPERSEDED by D-158** — the option was chosen on a framing that called ADIM 2's 59.5 % an over-allocation; Barış then stated it is deliberate. |
| D-156 | 2026-08-05 | **The block band subdivides into 50 rows of 13.00 pt, and per-block capacity is a hard ceiling, published in `TEMPLATE_ANALYSIS.md` §12.5.** Chart-capable blocks (≥10 canvas rows, the shipped `CHART_ROW_SPAN`): ADIM 1, 2 and 4 only. Ceilings at the median 6-row entry: ADIM 1 → 2 entries or 1 chart; ADIM 2 → 3 entries or 1 chart + 3 entries; ADIM 3 → 1; ADIM 4 → 2 or 1 chart + 1 entry; ADIM 5/6/7 → 1 each; ADIM 8 → 0 at 6 rows, 1 at a lean 4-row entry. Text wraps at 128 characters per line at 8 pt across the 567 pt canvas. | 13.00 pt divides 650 exactly and gives 8 pt type 1.63× leading; Rev00's 18 pt row was measured for 11 pt type and, at D-146's 1:1 authoring where the floor is 8 pt, wastes 38 % of every row — the row height, not the block split, is the dominant capacity lever (36 rows/column at 18 pt vs 50 at 13 pt, same paper). Every constant here is read from shipped code rather than estimated: `CHART_ROW_SPAN = 10` from `pareto`/`trend`/`distributionChart`, entry length from `fieldFormLines` over the real 3–6 field methods, one wrapped line per content row from D-100, 128 chars from `estimateCharsPerLine`. **The consequence that matters for Oturum B: five blocks share the right column's 650 pt, so `CHART_ROW_SPAN = 10` does not fit ADIM 5/6/7 (6 canvas rows) or ADIM 8 (5) — "a full-height chart in every step" is geometrically impossible on this sheet, and option C exposed that rather than solving it.** | LOCKED |
| D-157 | 2026-08-05 | **P-29 is closed: `Rev00` IS the form Farplas approves, so D-10's default template moves to the Rev00-based 8-step template once it exists.** `farplas-7step-tr` is demoted from "the company standard we default to" to a legacy-compatibility template — it stays in the registry so existing 7-step A3s still open and export, but it is no longer what a new project starts on. D-10's *rationale* is untouched and is precisely what forces the change. | Barış's answer. D-10 (LOCKED) reads "an app that outputs a non-standard A3 does not get adopted"; that argument pointed at `farplas-7step-tr` only while Rev00's status was unknown — its title reads `OTOMOTİV PPS`, not Farplas-branded, and its header field set differs from §3's, which is why P-29 was raised rather than assumed. With Rev00 confirmed as the approved form, the same adoption argument now points the other way. Operationally nothing changes today: D-95 means `farplas-7step-tr` is still the only template that exists, and the Rev00 template is authored in Phase 11 — this decision fixes which one Phase 11 makes the default, before any template file is written, which is the deadline P-29 itself set. | LOCKED |
| D-158 | 2026-08-05 | **Supersedes D-155. The block split is ELASTIC within a fixed column total, not a fixed template constant.** Invariant: each column is exactly 50 rows = 650.00 pt, so the block band's top and bottom edges are identical in both columns and can never drift — Barış's binding requirement. Elastic: the boundaries *between* steps move with content. What the template stores is therefore a **default** and a **per-block minimum**, not a fixed height. Defaults (rows of 50): left 14 / 28 / 8 (ADIM 1/2/3), right 20 / 8 / 8 / 8 / 6 (ADIM 4/5/6/7/8). Minimums (relaxed same day per D-160): 12 / 20 / 5 and 14 / 6 / 6 / 6 / 5 — leaving 13 elastic rows on each side, and letting ADIM 2 reach 33 rows (66 %, above Rev00's own 59.5 %) when a complex problem needs it. | Two corrections from Barış, both domain knowledge this session did not have. (1) **ADIM 2 and ADIM 4 are deliberately large** — "en çok giriş o alanlara giriliyor" — so §11.3's 59.5 % is a design choice, not the over-allocation §6 criticised in Template A, and D-155's framing (mine) was misleading. Defaults now sit within 3.5 points of Rev00's own proportions everywhere. (2) **The top and bottom lines must never drift, but the interior may breathe** — which changes the *kind* of thing a block budget is: a layout-time allocation with a fixed total rather than a static constant. That dissolves most of D-155's tension, since a starved block can borrow from a column-mate that does not need its default. ADIM 1's default rises 12 → 14 rows because it now carries three mandatory panels (D-159), and ADIM 2 pays the 1.5 points for it — a concrete content requirement, not a judgement about balance. | LOCKED |
| D-159 | 2026-08-05 | **ADIM 1 carries three mandatory panels: 5N1K analysis, gap analysis, and problem statement.** Barış's requirement, taken from the P-32 worked example (which has gap analysis + problem statement) plus 5N1K, which he requires unconditionally. Measured fit against D-158's 14-row default (12 canvas rows = 156 pt over 567 pt): a 4-row 5N1K strip of six 94.5 × 52 pt cells, above an 8-row pair of 283.5 × 104 pt panels for gap analysis and problem statement. It fits exactly, with no slack. | Recorded here rather than designed here: *what must be present* is a page-contract input because it sets ADIM 1's minimum height, but *how each panel renders* is Oturum B. The exact fit is the point — if any of the three grows, D-158's elastic allocation must take rows from ADIM 2 or 3, and there is no hidden slack to absorb it silently. `five-g-5n1k` already ships as a Step 1 plugin (Phase 5); `gapStatement` ships from 6a. The problem-statement panel's four colour-coded bands (ultimate goal / ideal / current / problem) have no plugin yet. | LOCKED |
| D-160 | 2026-08-05 | **The user may grow ADIM 2 at ADIM 3's expense, down to a floor of 5 rows — but ADIM 3 can never reach zero. Allocation is deterministic; AI advises on content, never on layout.** Minimums relaxed so the elasticity is real: ADIM 3 floor 5 rows (65 pt — header + label + a 3-row SMART/baseline/target strip of 189 × 39 pt zones), ADIM 1 floor 12. ADIM 2 can therefore reach 33 rows (66 %), above Rev00's own 59.5 %. | Barış: on a complex problem the user will want to trade Step 3's area for Step 2's, and being forced into fixed boxes is exactly what users hate. D-158's elastic model already allows this — the floors were simply set too tight. **Shrinking is not dropping:** D-11 and `CLAUDE.md` lock Step 3 as mandatory because it is what separates Toyota's 8 steps from Imai's 7 and it is the step practitioners silently skip, so the floor is the guard that keeps the method intact while giving Barış the flexibility he asked for. **On the AI question, deliberately no:** the allocation is arithmetic — each block declares a row demand, free rows go to unmet demand, floors hold, the column總 stays 50 — so a deterministic solver is simpler, testable and stable, and putting a model in the layout path would break `CLAUDE.md`'s "the app is fully functional with AI off" (layout would stop being deterministic, and D-97's golden-file/fidelity tests would lose their meaning). The AI's real role here is the one already specified: propose what to *condense* when demand exceeds supply ("these three Step 2 entries could merge into one stratification table"), through the existing propose-and-Accept path — never move a boundary itself. Nothing is lost either way: D-100 already routes any entry that does not fit to an appendix sheet rather than truncating it, so the constraint decides what is on the A3 *face*, which is the discipline of A3 thinking, not a data limit. | LOCKED |

B1 and B2 are only loosely coupled — B1's per-step data model is useful context for B2 but does not gate it — so splitting costs nothing structurally. B2's own launch prompt lives at `docs/oturumlar/B2-gorsel-dil-arayuz.md`, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "session launch prompts live in `docs/oturumlar/`, not in chat." | LOCKED |
| D-162 | 2026-08-06 | **ADIM 1's problem-statement panel stays 3 fields (Ideal / Actual / Gap), matching the already-shipped `gapStatement` schema exactly — no 4th "ultimate goal" field, no schema change.** Only `gapStatement`'s `renderToA3` gains a colour-band treatment (B2's job); `GapStatementPayloadSchema` is untouched. | Barış's call, via AskUserQuestion: "Ideal durumla ultimate goal aynı şey değil mi? ... 3 alanlı bırakamaz mıyız?" ## Oturum B2 — blok görsel dili, adım arayüzü, esnek tahsis, 2026-08-06

`docs/oturumlar/B2-gorsel-dil-arayuz.md`'nin §5.2–§5.4'ünü yürüten oturum (D-149's dördüncüsünün
ikinci yarısı, D-161'in B1/B2 bölmesi). No code — measurement/decision session, mirroring B1.
Full write-up: `reference/TEMPLATE_ANALYSIS.md` §14.

| D-165 | 2026-08-06 (v2 hex ONAYLANDI 2026-08-16) | **The 5N1K/LeanUK palette collision is resolved by extension, not neutralisation — two independent, zero-overlap colour layers rather than one merged palette.** Layer A (goal-state, LeanUK-derived, 3 colours): green=ideal (`#8FBF4F`), blue=actual (`#4A90D9`), red=gap (`#E0342A`) — already committed by D-162 for `gapStatement`'s render. **These are the v2 values**, confirmed via D-171's Block Visual Verification Loop after ADIM 1's first mockup round found the original darker fills (`#3F7D4A`/`#2D6FA3`/`#B23A3A`) needed to be lighter (for black-text legibility) and paired with a gap bracket/arrow/callout and separated rounded Problem Statement cards — none of which was visible from the geometry tables alone. Layer B (5N1K category, 6 new colours, none reused from Layer A): amber (Ne?), violet/`--color-accent` (Neden?), teal (Nasıl?), rose (Kim?), sienna (Ne zaman?), slate (Nerede?). Layer B is realised as a **static template cell style** (same mechanism as the legacy template's `blockHeaderPlan`/`Do`/`Check`/`Act` fills, D-101) since the six leaves' identity and position are fixed by the plugin's own schema — no new field on `A3TextLine`/`A3ContentZone` is needed. | Barış chose "genişlesin" over the recommended "nötrleştir" in an `AskUserQuestion`. Neutralising would have satisfied "düzenli ve yalın" cheaply but discarded 5N1K's own visual character, which Barış explicitly wants kept. Extension resolves the *collision* (no hue carries two meanings in the same block) without erasing either source's identity. Deliberately excludes yellow and cyan from Layer B — D-47 (LOCKED) already claims them for the PDCA header-tint system (`--color-plan`/`do`/`check`/`act`), and while red/green's reuse across the header-tint and goal-state layers is judged a deliberate thematic echo (Plan↔Problem, Act↔Ideal) rather than a collision, adding a *third* independent meaning-system on the same two hues was judged one echo too many. | LOCKED |
| D-166 | 2026-08-06 | **ADIM 1 final shape: D-159's three mandatory panels are unchanged (5N1K strip 4 rows/six `zones` cells; gap analysis + problem statement are two `zones` renderings of the *same* `gapStatement` entry, mirroring LeanUK's own "1.1 Gap Analysis chart / 1.2 Problem Statement bands" pairing). Two further methods — 5G (`five-g-5n1k`, shipped) and a new `problem-impact` plugin (Pareto reusing the existing `pareto-chart` imageKind + a fixed-field monthly/yearly cost-loss form) — are optional ADIM 1 entries with no guaranteed panel, placed via the elastic-allocation mechanism (D-158/§14.7) and D-100's appendix-overflow guarantee like any other entry.** | Mid-session, Barış shared a hand-annotated screenshot proposing a two-column ADIM 1 (left: problem definition; right: Pareto + monthly/yearly financial-loss impact) and asked for a direct opinion. Two `AskUserQuestion` rounds confirmed: (1) D-159's single-column, zero-slack geometry is **not** reopened — the sketch was a concept note, not a literal geometry proposal; (2) the Pareto/cost-impact content becomes a **new, independent Step 1 plugin** (`problem-impact`) rather than a reference into ADIM 2's existing `pareto` entries — consistent with D-124's "one traceable node = one Entry" and D-132's own "add alongside, don't reverse" precedent. This also gives 5G a more honest disposition than the earlier "always to appendix" framing: both 5G and `problem-impact` are ordinary optional entries, subject to the *same* elastic-allocation/appendix rules as any other Step 1 content — not a special-cased exile. | LOCKED |
| D-167 | 2026-08-06 | **P-31 answered per-block content, not with one blanket mechanism. ADIM 5/6/8 need no new mechanism** — their content is table/log text (`countermeasure`, `actionItem`, the still-unbuilt Step 8 methods); the only addition is applying D-41's *already-shipped* shape-coded status marker inline per row, tinted with Layer A. **ADIM 7 gets the one new mechanism this session recommends: `A3ImageKind = "kpi-strip"`**, a `KpiStripChartSpec` (`{ label, baseline, target, actual, unit }[]`) sized to the 567×78 pt box, using Layer A colour to signal on-target/in-progress/behind. | The registry scan run this session found Step 7 has **zero** dedicated plugins today (only `genericText`) — confirming B1's §13.1 finding (the reference form's `Data Analysis` sheet genuinely tracks KPI baseline/target/actual/phase) and making ADIM 7 the only one of the four "tracking blocks" that actually needs a compact chart. Forcing the same KPI-strip treatment onto ADIM 5/6/8 would have been dishonest to their real (tabular) content and would have spent a second new mechanism this slice does not need — D-114's "one mechanism per slice" discipline is honoured by scoping `kpi-strip` to whichever future slice (6d) builds Step 7's plugins. | LOCKED |
| D-168 | 2026-08-06 | **ADIM 3's visual language is untouched — D-38's three-zone strip (already implemented in the shipped `smartTarget` plugin) is confirmed to degrade correctly under D-160's elastic floor (5 rows), since `placeZones.ts`'s percentage-based `widthFraction` already scales with the reserved row-span. ADIM 2 and ADIM 4 need no new mechanism either** — every method registered to either step already emits `lines` and/or an existing `A3ImageKind` (`pareto-chart`/`trend-chart`/`distribution-chart` for Step 2; `fishbone-diagram` for Step 4's diagram methods) under D-99/D-102's shared contract. | Verified by reading the real registry (`getMethodsForStep`), not assumed — Step 2's ten methods and Step 4's nine all already conform to the one shared `A3BlockContent` shape. The open problem in these two blocks is not their visual grammar but their *picker* (too many equal-weight cards, §5.2/D-169) — conflating the two would have meant redesigning something that already works. | LOCKED |
| D-169 | 2026-08-06 | **`MethodPlugin` gains an optional `tier?: "recommended" \| "more"` field (unset = `"more"`, backward compatible). `MethodBand` renders two sections: a prominent "Recommended" row shown by default, and a collapsed "Other formats" list/disclosure.** Starting `"recommended"` assignments are an editorial default, not a frozen list: `gapStatement`+5N1K+`fiveW2H` (Step 1), `stratificationMatrix`+`categoryBreakdown`+`pareto`+`trend` (Step 2), `smartTarget` (Step 3, sole method), `fishbone`+`fiveWhy` (Step 4, D-11's canonical TBP framing), `countermeasure`+`weightedDecisionMatrix` (Step 5), `actionItem` (Step 6); Steps 7–8 have none yet (only `genericText` is registered there today). | The registry census run this session measured the real shape of the problem `B-adim-anatomisi.md` only estimated: Step 2 alone carries 10 dedicated methods, Step 4 carries 9, and `MethodBand.tsx` (read, unmodified) renders every step's methods as one flat, equal-weight wrapped grid with no ordering — textbook Anayasa G6 ("tasarlanmamış yüzey"). | D-170 | 2026-08-06 | **The elastic-allocation interface (D-164) resolves as: the deterministic solver (D-158/D-160) always runs automatically from declared row demand; a manual drag-handle at each block boundary sets a `pinned` row count that becomes a constraint the solver treats as fixed rather than computed, re-solving the remaining blocks around it within D-160's floors. A block at its floor shows a badge/disabled-grow-handle; a `pinned` block shows an "reset to automatic" control; D-100's appendix-overflow indicator (existing `a3Visibility`, shown on `EntryRow`) stays the visible signal when even a grown/pinned block can't fit everything.** | Answers D-160's own open question ("how does the user see a block is at its floor") concretely rather than leaving it to a future session to improvise. `pinned` as a per-block override (not a raw height override) keeps the mechanism deterministic and testable exactly the way D-160 requires — a pin is still just one more constraint the same arithmetic solver honours, not a second layout code path. | LOCKED |

Read as a presentation choice, not a data-model requirement: the flat strip is what fits D-159/§14.2's zero-slack 4-row/94.5pt-per-cell budget without reopening the LOCKED page geometry, and it already matches `5N-1K.jpeg`. Barış confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` (recommended option chosen without pushback). Separately, the same document's ADIM 2 "2026 İlk 7 Aylık Kayıp" cost box and its VAKA BİLGİLERİ header table are strong real-world confirmation (no action needed) of D-166's `problem-impact` plugin and D-153's header design, both of which were speculative when locked. | The wheel vs. strip question is exactly the kind of tension the Block Visual Verification Loop (D-171) exists to surface early, before a mockup round gets built on an assumption a real example would have contradicted. Recorded now rather than only in chat so a future B3 session (ADIM 2 onward) doesn't have to re-ask it. | LOCKED |

`npm test`/`cargo test` unaffected — no code touched this session (measurement/decision only,

## Oturum D2b — block alignment fix + countermeasure priority scoring, 2026-08-18

| D-190 | 2026-08-18 | **D2b's own P-43 fix shipped in `src/a3/layout/placeZones.ts`, both design choices Barış's own (`AskUserQuestion`, both recommended options): Kusur 1 — a zone's line stack spreads across up to `rowsUsed = min(maxLinesAcrossZones, availableRows)` physical rows (one line per row when the band has room, falling back to today's single-row `\n`-join exactly when it doesn't — `smart-target`'s single 153.75 pt row), remaining rows left blank (top-aligned, "Seçenek A"); Kusur 2 — a zone assigned less than 50% of its own requested width (`MIN_ZONE_WIDTH_RATIO`) borrows one column at a time from whichever neighbour is wider, until it clears the threshold or no neighbour can give one up without itself emptying ("Seçenek C" — algorithm unchanged, only a post-hoc widen pass added).** `greedyAssignColumnIndices` now returns column *indices* (not keys) so `widenStrandedZones` can adjust boundaries before `toColumnRange` converts to the final shape — `splitColumnsIntoZones`'s public behaviour (and D-107's "every zone gets ≥1 column, else drop the entry" rule) is otherwise unchanged. `placeZoneLines` chunks each zone's lines into `linesPerRow = ceil(lines.length / rowsUsed)`-sized groups, one group per physical row; a chunk of exactly one line gets `entryLineStyleId(bold, tone)` (restoring per-line bold/tone fidelity a joined cell could never carry — five-n1k's bold question labels were silently losing their bold styling even before D-189's row-squish was fixed, an adjacent defect folded into this same edit rather than left half-fixed) — a chunk of more than one line keeps the pre-fix `ENTRY_BODY_STYLE_ID`, which is exactly what reproduces `smart-target`'s byte-identical output. **Verified, not assumed**: 7 new regression tests (`placeZones.test.ts` — both defects, directly, using `farplas-7step-tr`'s real B:O widths; `fiveN1K/xlsxSurvival.test.ts` — through the real registry/`buildA3Layout`), all RED-verified against the pre-fix code (temporarily restored via `git show HEAD:…`, run, reverted) before being trusted GREEN; `scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` regenerated and diffed **byte-identical** (`git diff` empty) — `smart-target` is one of its five real methods, so this is a real, not theoretical, proof that D-178's LOCKED visual survived untouched. **A second finding, folded into this session rather than filed separately**: the whole B3 cumulative artifact (all 8 ADIM mockups, D-165 v2 through D-179) was built against §12.8's still-unbuilt `pps-8step-auto` canvas (567 pt, 12 equal 47.25 pt columns, no legacy gutter columns at all) — never against `farplas-7step-tr`'s real, uneven, currently-*shipped* B:O geometry. D-189's two defects only manifest on the real template's uneven grid (confirmed: replaying `smartTarget`'s 0.32/0.4/0.28 fractions and `five-n1k`'s six equal fractions over the future template's clean 12-column grid never crosses the 50% borrow threshold and never lands a zone on a gutter column — there are none). This means the eight already-approved B3 mockups needed no re-verification (they were never testing the geometry this bug lives in), but it also means D-178's own "renderToA3.ts's real zone widths (181.44/226.80/158.76 pt)" description was, in hindsight, the *idealized future-canvas* proportional split, not `smart-target`'s actual currently-exported pt widths (490.5/530.25/373.5 pt, `farplas-7step-tr`'s real B:O) — a pre-existing, harmless-so-far gap between "what B3 verified" and "what ships today," surfaced rather than silently carried forward. A new artifact section, redeployed to B3's own cumulative URL rather than a new artifact, shows both methods' real `farplas-7step-tr` geometry (before/after for `five-n1k`, a zero-change confirmation for `smart-target`) — the actual re-verification P-43 asked for. | Two `AskUserQuestion` rounds, both before any code, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "write a short plan and let me approve it" and this being a change to a LOCKED (D-38/D-178) visual: Kusur 1's row-alignment choice (A over B, "üstten hizala" — matches `place.ts`'s own non-zone precedent, no new alignment concept) and Kusur 2's column-snapping fix (C over A/B — chosen after this session hand-simulated all three against `smart-target`'s real fractions and found only C leaves it untouched; A also happens to fix `five-n1k` but shifts `smart-target`'s G:L/M:O boundary by 18 pt as a side effect, and is only a statistical improvement, not a structural guarantee; B risked starving a zone entirely under a naive merge-then-run-original-greedy simulation). `npm test` 916/916 (up from 905/905 pre-session — 11 new: 7 for D-190, 4 for D-191 below), exit code 0 (checked via a separate logfile + `echo $?`, not piped through `tail`). `npm run lint` clean (the one pre-existing `ThemeProvider` warning). `npm run build` green (same pre-existing chunk-size warning). `cargo test` 89/89, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -- --check` all clean — Rust untouched, confirmed rather than assumed. | CLOSED 2026-09-07 — Barış's visual sign-off landed via L1's own BVVL round (D-223 madde 4, D-224) rather than a separate look at the redeployed B3 artifact; `smart-target`'s byte-identical fixture proof plus the real `pps-8step-auto` geometry Barış actually approved together close P-43/P-26's layout half. |
| D-191 | 2026-08-18 | **`countermeasure` (Step 5) gains Barış's "Uygulama Planı" prioritization table (`impactScore`/`costScore`/`durationScore`, each 1-5 "how favorable" — never raw magnitude — plus a manual `priorityDecision`), extending the already-shipped method per Barış's own choice (`AskUserQuestion`) rather than a new, separate Step 5 method.** Chosen over a standalone method because `countermeasure` already is Step 5's "one countermeasure = one entry" unit (D-124/6b) with `owner`/`targetDate` already present (Barış's own stated "sorumlu ve termin de kritik") and an existing multi-valued `rootCause` reference role (D-116/6b) — a second method would have needed its own parallel reference to the same root causes with no structural link between the two representations, D-71's own "two representations, no precedence rule" trap. `priorityScoreOf` (new `priorityScore.ts`, mirroring `impactEffortMatrix/quadrant.ts`'s `scoreValue` parsing exactly) multiplies the three scores; undefined (not zero) when any is blank/non-numeric, D-120's established posture. **The scoring direction was Barış's own worked example, read and confirmed rather than assumed**: all three axes score "how favorable," so a high-impact-but-expensive-and-slow countermeasure (mold modification, `Etki=5, Maliyet=1, Süre=1 → 5`) correctly scores low despite its impact, while a cheap, fast, high-impact one scores highest — every field label spells the direction out in the UI itself (`"Cost score (1-5, high = cheap)"`) so the convention holds even if this reading is ever revisited. `priorityDecision` is a **separate, always-manual** field from `status` — Barış's own second `AskUserQuestion` choice — never inferred from the score, the same "a human always makes the call, a formula only informs it" rule D-41's status glyphs already established project-wide (a low score can still be strategically pursued for a reason the formula can't see); defaults to `"pending"` (`emptyFieldFormValues`'s first-option convention). **Rendering**: the computed score gets its own summary line (`"Öncelik Puanı: {n}"`) carrying `priorityDecision`'s own D-41 glyph/tone (■ positive/● caution/▲ negative) — deliberately *not* the title line, since `status`'s glyph already owns that line and the two decisions (idea accepted vs. scheduled now) must never fight over one glyph slot; the score line is omitted entirely when any of the three inputs is blank, never shown as a fabricated `0`. The Editor shows the same computed score live, next to the form, mirroring `impactEffortMatrix/Editor.tsx`'s own live-quadrant pattern. **Backward compatibility, verified not assumed**: an entry persisted before these fields existed has them genuinely `undefined` at runtime (`Entry.payload` is never Zod-validated on load, D-52) — `renderToA3.ts`'s own `payload.priorityDecision` read is guarded with `?? ""` (the exact fix shape C1/D-180 already established project-wide for this bug class), proven by a dedicated regression test constructing a pre-migration payload directly rather than trusting the type system. KN ID/KÖ ID (the reference photo's own row-numbering columns) were deliberately **not** added as new stored fields — KÖ ID is the entry's own position in the step's entries band, and KN ID is already carried by the existing `rootCause` reference role; a new free-text duplicate of either would have been a second, driftable representation of a fact the app already tracks structurally. | Two `AskUserQuestion` rounds before any code, per `CLAUDE.md`'s "write a short plan and let me approve it": extend `countermeasure` vs. a new method (extend chosen, matches this file's own D-71/D-124 reasoning), and the pursue/abandon decision rule — fixed numeric threshold vs. relative rank vs. manual field (manual chosen, matching D-41's already-established "status is always a human call" precedent elsewhere in this codebase). `npm test` 916/916 (28 in `countermeasure/`, up from 17 — 4 new files/blocks: `priorityScore.test.ts` new, `schema.test.ts`/`renderToA3.test.ts`/`Editor.test.tsx` extended), exit code 0. `npm run lint`/`npm run build` clean. `cargo test`/`clippy`/`fmt` clean — Rust untouched, TS-only method-plugin change. `scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` not re-run — `countermeasure` is not one of the fixture's five real methods. | LOCKED |

## Oturum 6d — Steps 7-8's remaining plain methods + rounds/signOff bindings, 2026-08-18

| # | Date | Decision | Rationale | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-193 | 2026-08-19 | **Oturum 6e-1 shipped: the image-ingestion mechanism (D-118's own "numeric caps and exact IPC surface land with 6e") plus the two image-bearing methods needing no annotation (`gemba-observation-log`, `before-after-photos`).** The three annotation-needing methods (`defect-photo-board`, `spaghetti-diagram`, `value-stream-map`) plus the drawing-tool mechanism split off to 6e-2 (P-45) — Barış's choice via `AskUserQuestion`, the recommended option, matching D-138's own precedent for exactly this shape ("two unproven mechanisms in one slice" risk). **Numeric caps** (`AskUserQuestion`, recommended option): original photo capped at 2400px long edge / JPEG quality 85, thumbnail at 400px / quality 80 — distinct from `SPEC.md` §8.7's 1568px AI-transport figure (a separate, not-yet-built path, Phase 8+), per D-118's own explicit line between the two. **Rust** (`src-tauri/src/images/`, new `image` 0.25.10 + `kamadak-exif` 0.6.1 dependencies): `ingest_image_bytes` — decode, read EXIF `Orientation` via `kamadak-exif` (`read_orientation`, defaults to `1`/identity when absent or malformed), apply the matching pixel transform (`apply_orientation`, all 8 EXIF orientation codes, verified against a hand-derived exact 2×2 pixel grid per code, mutation-checked), downscale preserving aspect ratio (never upscales), re-encode JPEG — re-encoding from decoded pixels is *how* EXIF is stripped unconditionally, verified structurally (no `"Exif"` byte sequence in the output) rather than merely asserted. New Tauri command `image_import` (`ppsx/commands.rs`) reads `sourcePath` itself — raw photo bytes never cross into TypeScript — builds `assets/img_{id}.jpg`/`assets/thumb_{id}.jpg` entries (`id` generated by the caller via `crypto.randomUUID()`, the same convention `builders.ts` already uses, not a new Rust `uuid` dependency) and calls **`write_ppsx` completely unchanged** — D-64/D-67/D-91/D-92's hardened, tested full-replace write path was deliberately not touched or forked; the accepted cost is that image bytes keep round-tripping through the frontend's `otherEntries` on every ordinary autosave same as any other archive entry, same as before this decision. A crafted `imageId` smuggling a path-traversal segment is rejected by the *existing*, unmodified `is_safe_relative_name` check — proven with a dedicated test, not just claimed. **TypeScript wire shape**: `ImageRef` (`domain/model/entry.ts`) gains optional `role?: string`, mirroring `EntryReference.role` (D-116) — `before-after-photos`' two roled slots are the first user. `A3ImageRequest`/`PendingImageSlot` (`src/a3`) gain optional `source?: "asset"` + `assetImageId?: string` (D-118 point 4's own "source discriminant") — omitted/`"spec"` (the Phase 5/D-102 default) still means rasterize `kind`/`spec`; `"asset"` means the bytes already exist and `place.ts` computes identical geometry either way, only the composition root's resolver forks (new `src/a3/render/resolveAssetImages.ts`, `resolveAssetImagePlacements` — base64-encodes already-in-memory bytes directly, never touches `rasterizePendingImages`/`getA3ImageRendererMap`, per-image failure isolation mirroring D-109). `A3ImageKind` gains `"asset-photo"` so an asset-sourced request stays honestly typed rather than borrowing an unrelated chart kind. `ImagePlacement.mimeType` widens to `"image/png" \| "image/jpeg"` — verified via `rust_xlsxwriter`'s own docs that `Image::new_from_buffer` auto-detects JPEG from the buffer's signature and that `mime_type` was already an unconsulted `String` field in `writer.rs`, so **zero Rust changes** were needed for this half. `A3EntrySummary` (`methodContract.ts`) gains optional `images?: readonly A3EntryImageRef[]` (a structural mirror of `ImageRef`, not an import, keeping this file's zero-import purity) + `resolveA3Images` helper, mirroring `language?`/`resolveA3Language` exactly — every pre-existing hand-built `A3EntrySummary` test fixture keeps compiling; `place.ts` now forwards `entry.images` when building the summary it hands to `renderToA3`. **Generic shell UI** (`EntryImagesField.tsx`, beside `EntryReferenceField`/`EntryRoundField` in `EntryEditorDialog`, D-125's precedent applied a second time): a new declarative `MethodPlugin.imageSlots?: readonly MethodImageSlot[]` field (`{role?, labelKey, max}`, mirroring `referenceRoles`'s shape) lets one generic component serve both an unrolled repeatable slot and role-tagged fixed slots — no method renders its own upload UI, the same "declare, don't render" split D-116 established. New store action `importEntryImage` (`projectStore.ts`) deliberately knows nothing about entries/roles — it imports a photo into the currently-open `.ppsx` and returns an `ImageRef`, exactly like `saveNow` writes the whole project; **ordering is safety-critical and was a real design call, not incidental**: the Rust write (bytes durably on disk) always completes *before* the caller ever attaches the returned `ImageRef` to an entry's `images[]` — a failed `image_import` call therefore leaves nothing attached anywhere (clean retry, never a dangling reference), and `EntryImagesField` calls `saveNow()` immediately after attaching to close the (crash-only, harmless-orphan-not-dangling-reference) window before the next autosave would anyway. **Removing a photo only unlinks the `ImageRef`** — the underlying asset bytes stay in the `.ppsx` as an orphan, deliberately the same "leave it, never build a garbage collector this slice" posture D-117 already established for dangling entry references, not a new gap. **Product interpretation of SPEC.md §1.3's plural "photos"** (Gemba): one entry = one photo (`imageSlots: [{max: 1}]`), matching D-124's own "one traceable node = one Entry" precedent — multiple observations become multiple Gemba entries, not one entry with a multi-image zone strip; this was a deliberate simplification (capability-matched: the editor never shows more photos than the export can place) over building a dynamic-width zones layout for an unbounded photo count. `gemba-observation-log`'s photo (when present) reserves `PHOTO_ROW_SPAN = 10`, matching Pareto/Trend/`distributionChart`/`problem-impact`'s own Step 2 convention exactly. **`before-after-photos`** uses a static two-zone strip (D-38/`smartTarget`'s own pattern — a fixed, known-in-advance zone count, not `gemba`'s avoided dynamic shape), one zone per role, each zone's `image` present only when that role's photo exists; its payload schema is empty (`z.looseObject({})`) — SPEC names no field beyond the pair itself, and the entry's own `title` (present on every entry) already carries the caption, so a second redundant text field would have been speculative. Both new methods' `Editor`s therefore own no image UI at all — `gemba`'s owns its four `fieldForm` fields, `before-after`'s owns nothing and renders a one-line hint instead of an empty box. `IMAGE_FILTER` (`EntryImagesField.tsx`) deliberately excludes HEIC — the `image` crate has no HEIC/HEIF decoder without a separate `libheif` system dependency, out of this slice's budget — filed as P-44, a real, concrete, user-facing gap (iPhones default to HEIC) rather than silently left for a confusing Rust decode error to surface later. | Reusing `write_ppsx` unchanged rather than partitioning `other_entries` into Rust-owned vs. frontend-owned classes (the alternative that would let ordinary autosaves stop re-sending image bytes) was the one real engineering trade-off this slice made without a separate `AskUserQuestion` round — changing the contract of the one already-hardened, heavily-tested security-critical write path (D-64/D-67/D-91/D-92) for a performance concern with no evidence yet of being a real bottleneck is exactly the kind of unforced risk D-114's "one new mechanism per slice" cap exists to prevent; revisit only if real project sizes make the repeated-bytes cost measurably painful. Structural-mirror types (`A3EntryImageRef` beside `ImageRef`, rather than importing `ImageRef` into `src/a3`) preserve `methodContract.ts`'s existing zero-import purity, the same reasoning `place.ts`'s own comments already give for keeping `spec: unknown` opaque at the layout level. Gemba's one-photo-per-entry reading was chosen specifically to avoid a second, unproven layout mechanism (dynamic-width zones) inside a slice whose own budget was already the ingestion pipeline — matching this project's own repeated lesson (D-105/D-107/D-111/D-113/D-136) that the `place.ts`/`rasterize.ts` layer is the one place two untested mechanisms compounding has burned real sessions before. `npm test` 1040/1040 (244 files, up from 995/995 at 236 — 45 new: 23 across the two new method directories' schema/Editor/renderToA3/xlsxSurvival tests, 22 across the ingestion mechanism's own domain-selector/`src/a3`/store/IPC layers), exit code 0 (checked via a separate logfile, not piped through `tail`, per D-143's own repeated lesson). `npm run lint` clean (the one pre-existing `ThemeProvider` warning). `npm run build` green (same pre-existing chunk-size warning). `cargo test` 103/103 (up from 89/89 — 14 new across `src-tauri/src/images/`'s orientation/ingest modules and `image_import`'s own command tests), `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -- --check` both clean. `scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` regenerated and diffed **byte-identical** — neither method it exercises (Pareto/Trend/Fishbone/SMART Target) is asset-sourced, confirming this slice's additions are genuinely additive. | LOCKED |
| D-192 | 2026-08-18 | **D-114's Phase 6 slice 6d shipped: five plain Step 7/8 methods plus the one new mechanism it names — binding `rounds`/`signOff` (D-58, defined since Phase 2, never previously read or written by any interface) into the app.** §2.1's own verification table was resolved by reading the shipped registry line by line rather than trusting the slice sketch, confirming two of SPEC.md §1.3's Step 7 bullets were already fully covered and needed no new code: "Result KPI chart" + "Before-Target-After comparison card" both by `kpiStrip` (D-167/D-177), and "Process confirmation audit" by `sustainmentAudit` (D-183) — the latter's own 12 columns (audit date, area/line, standard checked, auditor, finding, next audit…) already are exactly "was the countermeasure executed as designed? date, auditor, findings." Adım 8's "Sustain plan" is a genuinely different, forward-looking artefact from `sustainmentAudit`'s own backward-looking log (which audit *type*/frequency/owner/LPA linkage governs *future* audits, vs. one completed audit event), so it got its own new method rather than being folded in. **Five new plain methods, zero new mechanism of their own** (`shared/fieldForm.ts`/`rowTable.ts`, D-127/D-115): Step 7 — `statistical-confirmation` (Cp/Cpk/p-chart summary/defect rate), `realized-cost-benefit` (realized benefit/actual cost/net benefit/notes), `result-verdict` (a `verdict` select — pending/met/partiallyMet/notMet — plus notes); Step 8 — `sustain-plan` (audit type/frequency/owner/LPA linkage), `open-items-next-problem` (a row table: description/owner/target date/status). `result-verdict`'s `pending` default (not one of SPEC's own three real values) and `open-items-next-problem`'s status glyph (open=▲negative/closed=■positive, mirroring `implementationIssuesLog`'s already-shipped two-valued pattern exactly) are both mechanical reuses of D-183/D-180's own established conventions, not new design calls. **The rounds/signOff mechanism itself, all three of its real design questions resolved via one `AskUserQuestion` round before any code, all three of Barış's recommended options confirmed:** (1) Adım 7's "Verdict: target met/partially met/not met → if not met, 'Return to Step 4' loop" (SPEC.md §1.2 S7) is a plain field plus a **manual** "Yeni analiz turu başlat" control — not automatic navigation, since Phase 7's readiness/gate state machine (D-85) doesn't exist yet and building a fragment of it here would have been exactly the kind of "second new mechanism in one slice" D-114's own cap exists to prevent. (2) A round opens only via that manual control (`buildOpenRoundCommand`, closes any still-open round first, D-58's own "at most one open round" implicit rule made explicit); Steps 4-7 keep showing **every** entry unconditionally, no round filter — matching D-58's own "no snapshot, one entry list" design exactly — with an **optional** per-entry round tag (`EntryRoundField`, `EntryEditorDialog`'s generic shell UI, the same architectural slot `EntryReferenceField`/D-125 already established: a method's `Editor` never sees `roundId`, since `MethodEditorProps` is `{payload, onChange}` only, no `dispatch`/`project` access). (3) `signOff` lives in a new `SignOffPanel`, Step 8's own page only (`StepPage.tsx`'s first-ever per-step conditional render, `{stepId === 8 && <SignOffPanel />}` beside `{stepId === 7 && <RoundsBand />}`) — matching §1.3's own "Closure & sign-off" bullet sitting inside Step 8, not a project-level always-visible surface. **New commands, the first ever without a `stepId`**: `rounds.set`/`signOff.set` (`src/domain/commands/types.ts`), applied directly against `ProjectModel` (`applyCommand.ts` branches before routing into the existing step-scoped `applyToStep`, whose own parameter type was narrowed to `Exclude<Command, RoundsSetCommand \| SignOffSetCommand>` so the compiler — not a runtime check — proves every step-scoped case is still handled). `buildOpenRoundCommand`/`buildSetSignOffCommand` (`builders.ts`) follow the existing before/after-full-value shape `entries.reorder` already uses, no precondition check (same trust-the-caller posture `entry.update`'s own `before` already has). `Entry`'s existing `roundId?: string` (D-58, never before written by any builder) is threaded through `AddEntryInput`/`UpdateEntryInput` with the same three-state shape `nodeTree.ts`'s `parentId` already established for "explicitly none": `undefined`/omitted leaves it alone, `null` clears it, a string sets it. **Deliberately kept simple, not silently expanded**: both new command types stay `undoable: true` like every other command — `types.ts`'s own D-80 comment once floated a *non-undoable* sign-off command, but `dispatch()`/`undo()` don't actually consult the `undoable` flag anywhere today (confirmed by reading `projectStore.ts`), so wiring real non-undoable behavior would have been a second, unrelated dispatcher-level mechanism competing with this slice's one-mechanism budget; undoing a sign-off or a round-open is harmless in practice (nothing else derives from either irreversibly) and can be revisited if that ever stops being true. `EntryRoundField` only renders on Steps 4-7 (`ROUND_TAGGABLE_STEPS`) — the schema comment's own "step 4–7 entries opened under this round" scope, made an explicit UI constant rather than left implicit. New pure selectors `findOpenRound`/`roundOrdinal` (`src/domain/selectors/rounds.ts`) — array position is already creation order (`rounds` is append-only), so no separate ordinal is stored, matching `resequence`'s "position is authoritative" precedent one structure over. `scripts/gen-a3-fixture.ts` confirmed unaffected by direct read, not assumed: it already hardcodes `signOff: {}`/`rounds: []` and touches none of this slice's five new method ids, so the checked-in fixture needed no regeneration. | Model used: Sonnet 5 (this session), not the Opus D-28's own routing note suggested for §2.3's architecture-adjacent question — the three-way `AskUserQuestion` round carried the real judgment calls instead of requiring deep unassisted reasoning, and each of the three recommended options was confirmed without pushback, the same "low-risk question, quickly resolved" shape C1-C4's own sessions established. `npm test` 995/995 (up from 916/916 — 79 new: 22 domain/commands, 5 domain/selectors, 58 across the five new method directories' schema/Editor/renderToA3 tests, 14 across `EntryRoundField`/`RoundsBand`/`SignOffPanel`), exit code 0 (checked via a separate logfile + `echo $?`, not piped through `tail`, per D-143's own repeated lesson). `npm run lint` clean (the one pre-existing `ThemeProvider` warning). `npm run build` green (same pre-existing chunk-size warning). `cargo test` 89/89, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo fmt -- --check` all clean — Rust genuinely untouched: `roundId`/`rounds`/`signOff`'s wire shape was never changed, only which interfaces read/write already-existing optional fields. | LOCKED |

`problemImpact`'in `monthlyLoss`/`yearlyLoss` alanları finansal etki, gap'in kendisi değil. **Bu, session prompt'un öngörmediği yeni bir açık soru doğuruyor** (aşağıya bkz.). **S2** (veri-temelli girdi + PoC) — MEKANİK KONTROL EDİLEBİLİR: Step 2'de `pareto`/`trend`/`checkSheet`/`stratification-matrix` methodId'lerinden biri var mı + Step 2'de en az bir `point-of-cause` methodId'li entry var mı, ikisi de saf `entry.methodId` taraması, yeni alan gerekmiyor. **S3** (SMART hedef) — VAR, `smartTarget`'ın `metric`/`baseline`/`target`/`unit`/`dueDate` alanları doğrudan dolu-kontrolüne uygun. **S4** (kök neden verified) — kod taramasında `grep -rn "verified"` beş Step 4 kök-neden metodunda (`whyWhyTree`/`faultTree`/`causeEffectMatrix`/`fiveWhy`/`threeLeggedFiveWhy`) SIFIR sonuç verdi, ama session prompt'un öngördüğünden daha zengin bir tablo bulundu: `hypothesisVerification.rows[].verdict==="confirmed"` VE `whyWhyTree`'nin kendi `outcome==="confirmedRootCause"` alanı (D-184/P-35, Oturum C5'te eklendi) İKİ BAĞIMSIZ mevcut sinyal — `hypothesisVerification/columns.ts`'in kendi yorumu SPEC'in "verified = true" cümlesini birebir alıntılıyor ama hiçbir promosyon mekanizması yok. **S5** (karşı önlem doğrulanmış köke bağlı + hiyerarşi gerekçesi) — KISMEN VAR: `countermeasure.rootCause` referansı zaten var (D-116); "hiyerarşi gerekçesi" `errorProofingHierarchy.note` alanına + `level==="procedure"` (en zayıf, `levels.ts`) kontrolüne bakılabilir, ama bu çapraz-entry bir okuma (countermeasure'ın kendi alanı değil, ona `countermeasure` rolüyle bağlı ayrı bir `errorProofingHierarchy` entry'si). **S6** (aksiyon owner/dueDate) — VAR, `actionItem.owner`/`actionItem.dueDate` doğrudan mevcut. **S7** (process confirmation boş + Return-to-Step-4) — KISMEN VAR: `resultVerdict.verdict` (met/partiallyMet/notMet/pending) var; | D-194 | 2026-08-19 | **Oturum 6e-2 shipped: the annotation drawing mechanism (D-119's own storage/compositing design, previously locked but unbuilt) plus the three annotation-needing image methods (`defect-photo-board`, `spaghetti-diagram`, `value-stream-map`) — D-114's Phase 6 map (6a–6e) is now fully closed, and `SPEC.md`'s own Phase 6 plan is complete.** Three real open questions (§2.2 of `6e-2-goruntu-aciklama.md`) went to Barış via one `AskUserQuestion` round before any code, all three of the recommended options confirmed: hand-rolled SVG overlay over a canvas library (matching `KpiStripChart`'s own D-182 precedent, and avoiding a third-party DOM/canvas model inside the one capture path (`rasterize.ts`) already proven fragile three times — D-105/D-113/D-136); one shared `EntryAnnotationEditor.tsx` (D-125's generic-shell precedent applied a second time) rather than three per-method editors; one shape vocabulary (arrow/circle/callout/freehand path) shared by all three methods rather than method-specific subsets. **Data model** (`domain/model/entry.ts`): `AnnotationShapeSchema`/`AnnotationSchema` (`z.looseObject`, D-51's loose-schema posture — all fields but `id`/`shape` optional, since which are meaningful depends on `shape`, validated live by the editor rather than by a `z.discriminatedUnion`), normalized (0..1) `x0,y0`/`x1,y1`/`points`/`text` per shape; `ImageRef.annotations?` — additive, absent on every photo ingested before this session, no migration. **Export path, exactly as D-119 specified**: an entry's photo with `annotations.length > 0` becomes a `spec`-sourced `"annotated-photo"` slot (new `A3ImageKind`) routed through the existing D-102 rasterize path; an unannotated one keeps D-118's cheap direct `asset` path — the choice made automatically inside a new shared `renderAnnotatedPhotoBlock` (`src/methods/shared/annotatedPhoto.ts`), written once and called by all three methods' `renderToA3` (D-127's extraction-before-third-use discipline, here extracted before even the *first* use since three methods needed it from the start). **The one real architectural discovery this session made, not anticipated by D-119's own design**: `renderToA3`'s pure first pass never has access to a photo's actual bytes (only `A3EntryImageRef` — id/assetPath/role — never pixels), so an annotated image's `spec` can only ever carry a *reference* (`AnnotatedPhotoSpec = {assetImageId, annotations}`), never the photo itself — the composition root must resolve that reference to real bytes before rasterizing. New `resolveAnnotatedPhotoSpecs` (`src/a3/render/resolveAssetImages.ts`, beside the existing `resolveAssetImagePlacements`) does exactly this: looks up `otherEntries` the same way the asset path already does, replaces `spec` with a bytes-included `AnnotatedPhotoRenderSpec`, skips-and-logs an unresolvable reference (D-109's per-image failure isolation, once more) — `place.ts` itself never touches bytes, staying inside the D-43/D-94 purity boundary untouched. `a3Preview.ts` gained one line calling this before `rasterizePendingImages`. **A second, purely type-level discovery, real and worth recording since it will recur**: `Entry`/`ImageRef`'s Zod `z.looseObject` schemas infer a TypeScript type carrying an implicit index signature (`[x: string]: unknown`, how Zod expresses "preserve unknown keys," D-51) — a hand-written structural-mirror interface without one (`A3EntryAnnotation`, alongside the already-existing `A3EntryImageRef`) is *not* assignable in the direction domain-model-shape → mirror-shape when the mirror is the *target* of a write (only the read direction, mirror ⊇ domain in required fields, works for free). `AnnotatedPhotoCanvas` (the shared photo+overlay renderer, D-102/D-103's dual-mode pattern applied a second time after Fishbone — one component, `interactive` prop toggles live drawing vs. passive rasterize-mode render) standardizes on `A3EntryAnnotation` throughout its own props/state so both callers (the rasterizer, which only ever has `A3EntryAnnotation`, and `EntryAnnotationEditor`, which has real domain `Annotation`) can pass their value in without friction; the one unavoidable cast back into the domain shape happens once, at the true boundary (`EntryImagesField.handleAnnotationsChange`, writing into `Entry.images[].annotations`), documented with the same "safe by construction, same JSON either way" reasoning `registerMethod`'s own pre-existing erasure cast (`src/methods/types.ts`) already uses for an analogous type-level-only mismatch. **UI**: `MethodImageSlot` gains `annotatable?: boolean` (unset = not annotatable, the same "absent = not applicable" posture `referenceRoles`/`imageSlots` themselves already use); `EntryImagesField.tsx` renders an "Annotate" button per thumbnail on an annotatable slot, opening `EntryAnnotationEditor` in a widened `DialogContent` (`max-w-2xl` — the default `max-w-md` is too narrow for a photo canvas). `EntryAnnotationEditor` owns a 4-button tool toolbar (one always active, no separate "select" mode — removal is via a list, not click-to-select-on-canvas) and a keyboard-reachable list of placed shapes with per-item remove buttons — drawing itself is inherently pointer-driven (unavoidable for any canvas-based tool, the same "canvas is mouse-only, but every effect is separately reachable" posture `@dnd-kit` + explicit move buttons already established for entry reordering, D-86), but every shape's *removal* is keyboard/screen-reader accessible. **Three methods**, all following `before-after-photos`' precedent exactly (empty `z.looseObject({})` payload — SPEC names no field beyond the image itself for any of the three, the entry's own `title` carries the caption): `defect-photo-board` (Step 1, registers the shared `"annotated-photo"` `imageKind`/`renderImage` — the only one of the three that does); `spaghetti-diagram`/`value-stream-map` (Step 2, both reuse `defect-photo-board`'s registered renderer via `getA3ImageRendererMap()`'s kind-string dispatch, C2's own `problem-impact`-reusing-`pareto` precedent for this exact reuse shape, each proven end-to-end through the real registry in its own `xlsxSurvival.test.ts`). All three reserve `PHOTO_ROW_SPAN = 10`, matching Pareto/Trend/`distributionChart`/`problem-impact`/`gemba-observation-log`'s own established Step 1/2 convention. **An unrelated, one-line environment fix found and made along the way**: jsdom has no `PointerEvent` constructor at all (confirmed against the actual jsdom version in this repo) — `@testing-library/dom`'s `fireEvent.pointerDown/Move/Up` silently falls back to the base `Event` constructor, which drops `clientX`/`clientY` with no error, so any pointer-coordinate-driven component test would have measured `NaN` with zero visible symptom. Fixed once, in the shared `src/test/setup.ts` (a `MouseEvent`-subclassing polyfill, guarded by `if (!("PointerEvent" in window))`) rather than per-test — this will matter again the next time any component reads real pointer coordinates in a test. `RASTER_SCALE`'s slot-level-vs-global question (D-119's own flagged note) was **not** touched this session — an annotated photo's own capture size is driven by the block's `widthPt`/`heightPt` box exactly like every other rasterized image already, and this slice introduced no new evidence that the existing global `3` is wrong specifically for photos; left as `RASTER_SCALE`'s own pre-existing note, not re-opened without a concrete size complaint to act on. | The type-bridging discovery (domain `Annotation` vs. mirror `A3EntryAnnotation`) was found empirically by running `npm run build` after `npm test` passed clean — `vitest` transpiles via esbuild and does not itself full-type-check, so this class of error is invisible to `npm test` alone and only surfaces at `tsc`; a reminder that "tests green" and "build green" are two different, both-necessary gates, not one gate checked twice (Anayasa Madde 6's own "is this real verification or just reassurance" question, resolved here in favor of real — the build genuinely caught something tests couldn't). Reusing `defect-photo-board`'s renderer for the other two methods rather than each registering its own (structurally identical) renderer keeps `getA3ImageRendererMap()`'s registry small and avoids three near-duplicate `renderImage` closures for one shared component — the same reasoning C2's `problem-impact` already established for `pareto`. `npm test` 1107/1107 (up from 1040/1040 — 67 new: 9 domain-model schema, 5 shared-substrate unit, 6 `AnnotatedPhotoCanvas` render/interaction, 5 new `resolveAnnotatedPhotoSpecs` resolver tests (`resolveAssetImages.test.ts` grew 5→10), 34 across the three new method directories' schema/Editor/renderToA3/xlsxSurvival tests (14+10+10), 8 UI-wiring across `EntryImagesField`/`EntryAnnotationEditor`), exit code 0 (checked via a separate logfile, not mid-session, see the file's own git history rather than restating here). `npm run build` green (same pre-existing chunk-size warning). `cargo test` 112/112 (103 lib + 2 + 7, unchanged from D-193's own | P-15 | 2026-08-02 | **D-77's `expected_modified_ms` compare-and-swap is a narrowing of the concurrent-writer race, not a close of it** — empirically defeated by any mtime-preserving writer (sync clients, `rsync -t`, `robocopy /COPY:T`, a backup restore) and by ordinary coarse mtime granularity (FAT32: 2 s; common SMB shares: 1 s — exactly the shared-plant-drive threat D-06/D-77 name). | P-16 | 2026-08-02 | **TOCTOU window between `write_ppsx`'s mtime check and the atomic rename that commits it** — empirically confirmed: a concurrent write that lands inside the window (JSON serialization + deflate + temp-file write + fsync) is silently clobbered even though the check passed. Not corruption (`atomic_write`'s rename still guarantees no torn file — outcome is last-writer-wins, never worse than not checking at all), and today's autosave payloads are small enough that the real window is sub-millisecond. Grows with Phase 4's image payloads. Cheapest close: re-stat and re-compare immediately before the rename, which needs a pre-rename hook added to `atomic_write`. | Revisit once Phase 4 payload sizes make the window non-negligible |
